  "short_description": {
    "id": "Keteh Merirí adalah iblis tengah hari dalam tradisi Ibrani, disebut dalam Ulangan 32:24, yang menyerang pada terik siang dan membunuh siapa pun yang melihatnya.",
    "en": "Keteh Merirí is the noonday demon of Hebrew tradition, named in Deuteronomy 32:24, who strikes in the midday heat and kills whoever sees it.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Keteh atau Ketev Merirí, \"kehancuran yang pahit\", adalah iblis tradisi Ibrani yang disebut dalam Ulangan 32:24 dan dalam Mazmur sebagai wabah yang membinasakan di tengah hari. Ia dikenal sebagai \"Tuan Tengah Hari\" (Demonium Meridianum), iblis pembawa wabah dari gurun Yudea yang menyerang ketika matahari tepat di atas kepala, seperti Lilith menjelajah pada malam hari.",
      "en": "Keteh or Ketev Merirí, 'bitter destruction', is a demon of Hebrew tradition named in Deuteronomy 32:24 and in the Psalter as the plague that wastes at noonday. Known as the 'Lord of Noon' (Demonium Meridianum), he is a pestilential demon of the desert of Judea who strikes when the sun is overhead, as Lilith roams by night.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "keteh-meriri-c01",
        "keteh-meriri-c02",
        "keteh-meriri-c03",
        "keteh-meriri-c08",

      ]

    {
      "id": "Midrash menyebutnya Shed yang diciptakan pada malam Shabbat pertama, bukan sepenuhnya roh maupun jasmani. Tubuhnya bersisik, berbulu, dan penuh mata, dengan satu mata yang melihat di dalam jantungnya; versi lain menyebut tanduk di dahi dan mata di dada. Siapa pun yang melihatnya mati, sehingga orang dilarang berjalan sendirian pada siang hari selama tiga minggu antara 17 Tamuz dan 9 Av.",
      "en": "The midrash calls it a Shed created on the eve of the first Sabbath, neither fully spirit nor body. It is covered in scales, hair and eyes, seeing with one eye inside its heart; another version gives a horn on the forehead and an eye on the chest.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c01",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s1",
      "quote": "Ketev Merirí (Keteb Merirí o Quéteb Merirí) es el nombre de un demonio de la tradición hebraica, mencionado en el Libro del Deuteronomio 32:24",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketev Merirí (Keteb Merirí atau Quéteb Merirí) adalah nama iblis dalam tradisi Ibrani yang disebut dalam Kitab Ulangan 32:24.",
        "en": "Ketev Merirí (Keteb Merirí or Quéteb Merirí) is the name of a demon of Hebrew tradition, mentioned in Deuteronomy 32:24."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c02",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s1",
      "quote": "Se le identifica como al «Señor del Mediodía y de los Calurosos Veranos» (Demonium Meridianum); se trata de un demonio pestífero que habita el desierto de Judea",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia diidentikkan sebagai \"Tuan Tengah Hari dan Musim Panas yang Terik\" (Demonium Meridianum), iblis pembawa wabah yang menghuni gurun Yudea.",
        "en": "He is identified as the 'Lord of Noon and of Hot Summers' (Demonium Meridianum), a pestilential demon dwelling in the desert of Judea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c03",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s1",
      "quote": "Ketev Merirí ataca al mediodía, cuando el sol cae a plomo. Recorre las mismas regiones que Lilith, mortífero genio de la noche, recorre al oscurecer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketev Merirí menyerang pada tengah hari ketika matahari tepat di atas kepala, menjelajahi wilayah yang sama dengan Lilith, roh maut malam yang berkeliaran setelah gelap.",
        "en": "Ketev Merirí strikes at noon when the sun beats straight down, roaming the same regions that Lilith, the deadly night spirit, roams after dark."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c04",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s1",
      "quote": "En el Midrash Shojer Tov se menciona que este ser se trata de un \"Shed\", un depredador que fue creado la víspera de Shabbat en la creación del mundo; no es una criatura totalmente espiritual como los ángeles pero tampoco física como los seres humanos.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Midrash Shojer Tov menyebutnya \"Shed\", pemangsa yang diciptakan pada malam Shabbat saat penciptaan dunia, tidak sepenuhnya rohani seperti malaikat dan tidak pula jasmani seperti manusia.",
        "en": "The Midrash Shojer Tov calls this being a 'Shed', a predator created on the eve of the Sabbath at the world's creation, neither wholly spiritual like angels nor physical like humans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c05",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s1",
      "quote": "Está cubierto totalmente de escamas, de vellos y de ojos, por uno de sus ojos puede ver pero éste ojo se halla dentro de su corazón.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tubuhnya seluruhnya tertutup sisik, bulu, dan mata; ia dapat melihat dengan salah satu matanya, tetapi mata itu terletak di dalam jantungnya.",
        "en": "It is covered entirely in scales, hair and eyes; it can see with one of its eyes, but that eye lies inside its heart."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c06",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s1",
      "quote": "que hace referencia a este Shed –Keteh Merirí- quien gobierna en las tres semanas que median entre el 17 de Tamuz y el 9 de Av. No se debe, pues, caminar solo durante estos días desde la hora cuarta hasta la hora novena",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shed Keteh Merirí berkuasa pada tiga minggu antara 17 Tamuz dan 9 Av, sehingga pada hari-hari itu orang tidak boleh berjalan sendirian dari jam keempat sampai jam kesembilan.",
        "en": "The Shed Keteh Merirí rules in the three weeks between 17 Tammuz and 9 Av, so on those days one must not walk alone from the fourth hour to the ninth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c07",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s1",
      "quote": "Ketev Merirí es mencionado por Jorge Luis Borges en El libro de los seres imaginarios, en la entrada \"Demonios del judaísmo\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jorge Luis Borges menyebut Ketev Merirí dalam El libro de los seres imaginarios, pada entri \"Demonios del judaísmo\".",
        "en": "Jorge Luis Borges mentions Ketev Merirí in El libro de los seres imaginarios, in the entry 'Demonios del judaísmo'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c08",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s2",
      "quote": "основное значение слова — «разрушение». Слово «мерири» встречается всего один раз, и означает, скорее всего, «горечь». Таким образом, Кетев мерири — «горькое разрушение».",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Rusia, kata ketev berarti \"kehancuran\" dan meriri, yang hanya muncul sekali, kemungkinan berarti \"kepahitan\", sehingga Ketev meriri berarti \"kehancuran yang pahit\".",
        "en": "According to the Russian article, ketev means 'destruction' and meriri, found only once, most likely 'bitterness', so Ketev meriri means 'bitter destruction'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c09",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s2",
      "quote": "Также упоминается в Псалтири: «Не убоишься ужасов в ночи, стрелы, летящей днём, язвы, ходящей во мраке, заразы, опустошающей в полдень» (Пс. 90:5, 6)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga disebut dalam Mazmur: \"tidak takut kepada kengerian malam, panah yang terbang di siang hari, penyakit sampar yang berjalan di dalam gelap, dan wabah yang membinasakan di tengah hari\" (Mzm. 90:5, 6 dalam penomoran Septuaginta).",
        "en": "It is also mentioned in the Psalter: 'thou shalt not fear the terror by night, the arrow that flies by day, the pestilence that walks in darkness, the plague that wastes at noonday' (Ps. 90:5, 6 in the Septuagint numbering)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c10",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s2",
      "quote": "Внешне Кетев мерири — мохнат и страшен, во лбу у него — рог, глаз — на груди. Каждый, увидевший его, умирает (Мидраш Техилим 91, Бемидбар Рабба 12).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut midrash, Ketev meriri berbulu dan mengerikan, bertanduk di dahi dengan mata di dada, dan siapa pun yang melihatnya mati (Midrash Tehillim 91, Bemidbar Rabbah 12).",
        "en": "According to the midrash, Ketev meriri is shaggy and fearsome, with a horn on its forehead and an eye on its chest, and whoever sees it dies (Midrash Tehillim 91, Bemidbar Rabbah 12)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "keteh-meriri-c11",
      "source_id": "keteh-meriri-s2",
      "quote": "Контексты в Библии, где встречается Кетев мерири, намекают на связь с ханаанским божеством Решефом.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konteks Alkitab tempat Ketev meriri muncul mengisyaratkan hubungan dengan dewa Kanaan Resyef.",
        "en": "The biblical contexts where Ketev meriri appears hint at a link with the Canaanite god Resheph."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Marraco adalah naga atau momok penakut anak dari kota Lleida dalam mitos Katalan, bermulut cukup lebar untuk menelan manusia, kini diarak sebagai figur naga hijau dalam pesta kota.",
    "en": "The Marraco is a dragon or child-scaring bogey of Catalan myth in Lleida, with a mouth wide enough to swallow a person, now paraded as a green dragon figure at city festivals.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "marraco-c01",
      "marraco-c03",
      "marraco-c09"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lo Marraco adalah naga atau makhluk menakutkan dalam mitos Katalan di Lleida. Dalam cerita untuk menakuti anak, mulutnya cukup lebar untuk menelan manusia bulat-bulat; ia juga dikenal sebagai kadal sebesar naga yang memakan dot bayi sebagai lambang pertumbuhan. Nama marreco dipakai untuk momok serupa di Maestrat dan Menorca.",
      "en": "Lo Marraco is a dragon or fearsome creature of Catalan myth in Lleida. In tales to scare children its mouth could swallow a person whole; it is also known as a dragon-sized lizard that eats babies' dummies as a sign of growing up. The name marreco was used for a similar bogey in the Maestrat and Menorca.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "marraco-c01",
        "marraco-c02",
        "marraco-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal-usulnya diperdebatkan. Artikel bahasa Spanyol menyebutnya hewan totem dari abad ke-5 SM, tetapi artikel bahasa Katalan menyebut dugaan itu tidak berdasar karena totem kaum Ilerget adalah serigala. Figur naga dipakai dalam prosesi kota sejak 1551, dan menurut satu sumber properti tahun 1907-lah yang memberi makhluk ini bentuk dan warna. Figur sekarang, naga hijau tanpa sayap sepanjang 8,5 m yang mengeluarkan asap dari hidung, dibuat pada 1957.",
      "en": "Its origin is disputed. The Spanish article calls it a totem animal from the 5th century BC, but the Catalan article calls this baseless, since the Ilergetes' totem was the wolf. A dragon figure joined city processions from 1551, and one authority says the 1907 prop gave the creature its shape and colour. The present figure, a wingless green dragon 8.5 m long that blows smoke from its nostrils, was built in 1957.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "marraco-c04",
        "marraco-c05",
        "marraco-c06",
        "marraco-c07",
        "marraco-c08",
        "marraco-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "marraco-c01",
      "source_id": "marraco-s1",
      "quote": "The Marraco (Catalan: Lo Marraco, Catalan pronunciation: [məˈraku])  is a dragon or frightening creature in Catalan myth in the city of Lleida. It possessed a mouth wide enough to swallow a human whole according to tales told to frighten children.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Marraco (bahasa Katalan: Lo Marraco) adalah naga atau makhluk menakutkan dalam mitos Katalan di kota Lleida; menurut cerita untuk menakuti anak, mulutnya cukup lebar untuk menelan manusia bulat-bulat.",
        "en": "The Marraco (Catalan: Lo Marraco) is a dragon or frightening creature of Catalan myth in the city of Lleida; in tales told to frighten children, its mouth was wide enough to swallow a person whole."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c02",
      "source_id": "marraco-s3",
      "quote": "Amb el nom de lo Marraco es coneix a la ciutat de Lleida un lluert de la mida d'un drac, amb una boca especialment ampla i grossa amb la qual es menja els xumets dels infants, com a símbol del seu creixement.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Lleida, Lo Marraco dikenal sebagai kadal sebesar naga dengan mulut sangat lebar yang memakan dot bayi, sebagai lambang pertumbuhan anak.",
        "en": "In Lleida, Lo Marraco is known as a dragon-sized lizard with a very wide mouth that eats babies' dummies, as a symbol of their growing up."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c03",
      "source_id": "marraco-s3",
      "quote": "Lo Marraco és també un espantamainades tradicional de Lleida. Hi ha altres indrets on es feia servir el mateix nom (en la variant, però, marreco) per a fer por a la mainada, com ara el Maestrat i Menorca.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lo Marraco juga momok penakut anak tradisional Lleida; nama yang sama (dalam varian marreco) dipakai untuk menakuti anak di tempat lain seperti Maestrat dan Menorca.",
        "en": "Lo Marraco is also a traditional child-scarer of Lleida; the same name (as marreco) was used to frighten children elsewhere, such as the Maestrat and Menorca."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c04",
      "source_id": "marraco-s2",
      "quote": "El Marraco es un animal totémico de Lérida que data del siglo V a. C. Como animal mitológico no ha existido nunca y su representación es libre.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, Marraco adalah hewan totem Lérida dari abad ke-5 SM yang wujudnya bebas digambarkan.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, the Marraco is a totem animal of Lérida dating from the 5th century BC, freely represented."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c05",
      "source_id": "marraco-s3",
      "quote": "La veritat, però, és que tant aquestes conjectures com el fet d'atribuir al basc l'origen del nom (sovint es diu que \"marraco\" vol dir 'drac' en aquesta llengua) no tenen cap fonament històric ni científic. D'altra banda, l'animal totèmic dels ilergets era el llop, no pas el drac.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Katalan menegaskan bahwa dugaan asal-usul kuno itu, juga anggapan namanya berasal dari kata Basque untuk naga, tidak berdasar sejarah maupun ilmiah; hewan totem kaum Ilerget adalah serigala, bukan naga.",
        "en": "The Catalan article stresses that these ancient-origin conjectures, and the idea that the name comes from Basque for 'dragon', have no historical or scientific basis; the totem animal of the Ilergetes was the wolf, not the dragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c06",
      "source_id": "marraco-s1",
      "quote": "However, this is contradicted by a different authority, which notes that it was the advent of the marraco prop built in 1907 for the parade which \"gave shape and color to this imaginary creature\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber lain menyatakan bahwa properti marraco yang dibuat pada 1907 untuk pawai-lah yang \"memberi bentuk dan warna pada makhluk khayalan ini\".",
        "en": "Another authority notes that the marraco prop built in 1907 for the parade 'gave shape and color to this imaginary creature'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c07",
      "source_id": "marraco-s1",
      "quote": "One recorded post-medieval precursor of \"Lo Marraco de Lleida\" was the drac or dragon figure used by the city during the procession of the Assumption of Mary for the year in 1551.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu pendahulu Lo Marraco de Lleida yang tercatat adalah figur naga yang dipakai kota itu dalam prosesi Maria Diangkat ke Surga tahun 1551.",
        "en": "A recorded precursor of Lo Marraco de Lleida was the dragon figure the city used in the procession of the Assumption of Mary in 1551."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c08",
      "source_id": "marraco-s2",
      "quote": "La actual representación fue construida en 1957 por el arquitecto Lluís Domènech Torres.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Figur yang sekarang dibuat pada 1957 oleh arsitek Lluís Domènech Torres.",
        "en": "The present figure was built in 1957 by the architect Lluís Domènech Torres."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c09",
      "source_id": "marraco-s2",
      "quote": "Tiene un tamaño de 8,5 m de largo por 2,90 m de ancho y 3,75 de alzada. Su peso supera las dos toneladas. Es un dragón sin alas, de color verde con colmillos muy afilados y saca humo por la nariz.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Figur itu panjangnya 8,5 m, lebar 2,90 m, tinggi 3,75 m, dan beratnya lebih dari dua ton; berupa naga hijau tanpa sayap bertaring tajam yang mengeluarkan asap dari hidung.",
        "en": "The figure is 8.5 m long, 2.90 m wide and 3.75 m high and weighs over two tonnes; it is a wingless green dragon with very sharp fangs that blows smoke from its nose."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "marraco-c10",
      "source_id": "marraco-s1",
      "quote": "The Catalan word marraco (regional variant of marraix) also denotes a type of shark, and the corresponding Basque word marraxo meaning 'shark' appears to be borrowed from the Spanish marrajo, according to a linguistic authority.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Katalan marraco (varian regional marraix) juga berarti sejenis hiu, dan kata Basque marraxo (\"hiu\") tampaknya dipinjam dari bahasa Spanyol marrajo.",
        "en": "The Catalan word marraco (a regional variant of marraix) also means a kind of shark, and the Basque marraxo ('shark') appears borrowed from Spanish marrajo."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {

            "en": "This is baseless; the Ilergetes' totem was the wolf, and the Marraco's form came from the 1907 parade prop."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Munaciello atau Monaciello, \"biarawan kecil\", adalah peri usil berjubah biarawan dari cerita rakyat Napoli yang menyusup ke rumah lewat sumur, mencuri, tetapi juga memberi harta.",
    "en": "The Munaciello or Monaciello, 'little monk', is a mischievous robed sprite of Neapolitan folklore who slips into houses through wells, steals, but also gives treasure.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "monaciello-c01",
      "monaciello-c02",

      "monaciello-c04"
    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Munaciello, \"biarawan kecil\", adalah peri dari cerita rakyat lisan Napoli, lelaki pendek dan kekar berjubah panjang bertudung. Menurut banyak kisah ia penjaga saluran air dan sumur bawah tanah kota, sehingga dapat menyusup diam-diam ke rumah-rumah. Ia usil, mencuri barang berharga dan mengganggu ibu rumah tangga, tetapi juga muncul pada malam hari kepada orang yang sangat membutuhkan dan menuntun mereka ke harta. Kunjungannya tidak boleh diceritakan, agar musibah tidak datang.",
      "en": "The Munaciello, 'little monk', is a sprite of the oral folklore of Naples, a short, stocky man in a long hooded robe. Many tales make him keeper of the city's underground aqueducts and wells, so he can slip secretly into houses. He is mischievous, stealing valuables and pestering housewives, but also appears at night to people in dire need and leads them to treasure. His visits must not be told, or misfortune follows.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "monaciello-c01",
        "monaciello-c02",
        "monaciello-c03",
        "monaciello-c04",
        "monaciello-c13"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ada beberapa hipotesis asal-usul. Matilde Serao mencatat kisah anak cacat dari tahun 1445 yang didandani ibunya dengan jubah biarawan; tudung merah menjadi pertanda baik dan hitam pertanda buruk. Legenda lain menjadikannya pengelola sumur (pozzaro), dan kebiasaannya memang mirip pengangkut air Napoli kuno; hipotesis ketiga menganggapnya iblis kecil.",
      "en": "There are several theories of origin. Matilde Serao recorded the story of a deformed child of 1445 whom his mother dressed in a monk's habit; a red hood was a good omen and a black one a bad omen. Another legend makes him a well keeper (pozzaro), and his habits do resemble those of old Naples' water carriers; a third theory sees him as a little demon.",

      "en": "The saying 'Forse avrà il Monaciello in casa' is used for sudden fortune. He appears in Eduardo De Filippo's play Questi fantasmi! and the film The Hand of God (2021).",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c01",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s1",
      "quote": "Monacello (Italian: [monaˈtʃɛlːo]), or Munaciello (Neapolitan: [munaˈtʃjelːə]), is a sprite from the traditional oral folklore of Naples, Italy.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Monacello atau Munaciello (bahasa Neapolitan) adalah peri dari cerita rakyat lisan Napoli, Italia.",
        "en": "Monacello, or Munaciello in Neapolitan, is a sprite of the oral folklore of Naples, Italy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c02",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s1",
      "quote": "Its name means \"little monk\" in the Neapolitan language. It gets often described as a \"benevolent man, short, stocky, very spiteful\" and enjoys visiting houses in the Historic center of Naples. It is thought to wear a long robe with a broad hood on top of it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berarti \"biarawan kecil\" dalam bahasa Neapolitan; ia sering digambarkan sebagai \"lelaki baik hati, pendek, kekar, sangat usil\" yang suka mengunjungi rumah di pusat bersejarah Napoli, mengenakan jubah panjang bertudung lebar.",
        "en": "Its name means 'little monk' in Neapolitan; it is often described as a 'benevolent man, short, stocky, very spiteful' who likes visiting houses in Naples' historic centre, wearing a long robe with a broad hood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c03",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s1",
      "quote": "According to many tales, he was the caretaker (or \"pozzari\") of the city's underground aqueducts, canals, and water wells. Monaciello knew every twist and turn in these underground passages and could use the wells to secretly enter the piazzas and homes of Naples.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut banyak kisah, ia penjaga (pozzari) saluran air bawah tanah, kanal, dan sumur kota, yang mengenal setiap lorongnya dan memakai sumur untuk diam-diam masuk ke alun-alun dan rumah warga Napoli.",
        "en": "According to many tales he was the keeper (pozzari) of the city's underground aqueducts, canals and wells, who knew every passage and used the wells to slip secretly into the squares and homes of Naples."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c04",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s1",
      "quote": "Despite his mischievous nature—like stealing valuables and bothering housewives—Monaciello is mostly benevolent. He's known to appear at night to those in desperate need, leading them to hidden treasures or giving them his own valuables, sometimes the same ones he had stolen, without asking for anything in return.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Meski usil, misalnya mencuri barang berharga dan mengganggu ibu rumah tangga, Monaciello umumnya baik hati: ia muncul pada malam hari kepada orang yang sangat membutuhkan dan menuntun mereka ke harta tersembunyi atau memberi barang berharganya sendiri tanpa meminta balasan.",
        "en": "For all his mischief, such as stealing valuables and bothering housewives, Monaciello is mostly benevolent: he appears at night to people in desperate need and leads them to hidden treasure or gives them his own valuables, asking nothing in return."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c05",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s1",
      "quote": "There's even a saying about unexpected fortune: \"Forse avrà il Monaciello in casa\" (perhaps he has had the little Monk in his house).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada ungkapan untuk rezeki tak terduga: \"Forse avrà il Monaciello in casa\" (mungkin ada Monaciello di rumahnya).",
        "en": "There is a saying for unexpected fortune: 'Forse avrà il Monaciello in casa' (perhaps he has had the little monk in his house)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c06",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s1",
      "quote": "The habits of Monaciello resemble those of the water carriers of ancient Naples who worked in the numerous tunnels connecting the city's underground wells.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kebiasaan Monaciello mirip para pengangkut air Napoli kuno yang bekerja di terowongan penghubung sumur bawah tanah kota.",
        "en": "Monaciello's habits resemble those of the water carriers of old Naples who worked in the tunnels linking the city's underground wells."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c07",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s2",
      "quote": "Monaciello (O'Munaciello en napolitain), « le petit moine », est un nain du folklore napolitain.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Prancis menyebut Monaciello (O'Munaciello dalam bahasa Neapolitan), \"biarawan kecil\", sebagai kurcaci dari cerita rakyat Napoli.",
        "en": "The French article calls Monaciello (O'Munaciello in Neapolitan), 'the little monk', a dwarf of Neapolitan folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c08",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s3",
      "quote": "Secondo la prima, riportata tra gli altri da Matilde Serao nel suo Leggende napoletane (1881), il munaciello sarebbe un personaggio realmente esistito. L'origine andrebbe fatta risalire al 1445, durante il regno di Alfonso V d'Aragona",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut hipotesis pertama, yang antara lain dicatat Matilde Serao dalam Leggende napoletane (1881), munaciello adalah tokoh yang benar-benar ada, yang asal-usulnya berawal pada 1445 di masa pemerintahan Alfonso V dari Aragon.",
        "en": "According to the first hypothesis, reported among others by Matilde Serao in her Leggende napoletane (1881), the munaciello was a real person whose story goes back to 1445, in the reign of Alfonso V of Aragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c09",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s3",
      "quote": "La madre prese a vestirlo con un abito bianco e nero da monaco, sempre speranzosa in un miracolo, e questo fatto fu all'origine del nomignolo munaciello attribuitogli dal popolo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah itu, sang ibu mendandani anaknya yang kecil dan cacat dengan jubah biarawan hitam-putih sambil berharap mukjizat, dan inilah asal julukan munaciello dari warga.",
        "en": "In that story the mother dressed her small, deformed child in a black-and-white monk's habit, hoping for a miracle, which gave rise to the people's nickname munaciello."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c10",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s3",
      "quote": "In particolare, se il cappuccio dell'abito era di colore rosso, se ne traevano auspici di buon augurio, mentre la malasorte veniva associata al cappuccio nero.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tudung merah pada jubahnya dianggap pertanda baik, sedangkan tudung hitam dikaitkan dengan nasib buruk.",
        "en": "A red hood on his habit was taken as a good omen, while a black hood was linked to bad luck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c11",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s3",
      "quote": "Altra leggenda invece vuole che il munaciello fosse l'antico gestore dei pozzi d'acqua (il \"pozzaro\"), il quale riusciva (per la sua statura piccola) ad entrare nelle case passando attraverso i canali che servivano a calare il secchio.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda lain menyebut munaciello dahulu pengelola sumur air (pozzaro) yang karena tubuhnya kecil dapat masuk ke rumah lewat saluran untuk menurunkan ember.",
        "en": "Another legend makes the munaciello the old keeper of the water wells (pozzaro), who, being small, could enter houses through the shafts used to lower the bucket."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c12",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s3",
      "quote": "Vi è anche una terza ipotesi, che descrive il munaciello come un piccolo demone, dispettoso perché cattivo, anche quando lascia monete",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada pula hipotesis ketiga yang menggambarkan munaciello sebagai iblis kecil yang usil karena jahat, bahkan ketika ia meninggalkan uang logam.",
        "en": "A third hypothesis describes the munaciello as a little demon, spiteful because wicked, even when he leaves coins."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c13",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s3",
      "quote": "In nessuno dei tre casi suddetti bisogna però rivelarne la presenza: secondo il folclore napoletano, possono capitare disgrazie e sfortuna a chi rivela una visita del munaciello.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kehadirannya tidak boleh diungkapkan: menurut cerita rakyat Napoli, orang yang membocorkan kunjungan munaciello bisa ditimpa musibah dan kemalangan.",
        "en": "His presence must never be revealed: in Neapolitan folklore, misfortune can befall whoever tells of a visit from the munaciello."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c14",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s3",
      "quote": "Tra le più importanti si ricordano quella di Eduardo De Filippo nella sua opera Questi fantasmi!, in cui la commistione tra la leggenda del visitatore soprannaturale ed il grottesco della vita quotidiana viene espressa attraverso il personaggio dell'amante della moglie, che Eduardo scambia per un munaciello.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu rujukan sastra terpenting adalah drama Eduardo De Filippo, Questi fantasmi!, yang tokoh kekasih sang istri disangka seorang munaciello.",
        "en": "Among the most important literary references is Eduardo De Filippo's play Questi fantasmi!, in which the wife's lover is mistaken for a munaciello."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "monaciello-c15",
      "source_id": "monaciello-s1",
      "quote": "In the 2021 film The Hand of God, the Little Monk appears as a young boy as opposed to a short, older man.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam film The Hand of God (2021), Si Biarawan Kecil muncul sebagai anak laki-laki, bukan lelaki tua bertubuh pendek.",
        "en": "In the 2021 film The Hand of God, the Little Monk appears as a young boy rather than a short older man."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Asal-usul munaciello",
        "en": "Origin of the munaciello"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Anak cacat nyata dari tahun 1445 (Matilde Serao).",
            "en": "A real deformed child of 1445 (Matilde Serao)."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

            "monaciello-c09"
          ]
        },
        {

            "en": "A well keeper (pozzaro)."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Iblis kecil.",
            "en": "A little demon."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

          ]

    }
  ],
  "gaps": [

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Pesanta adalah anjing atau kucing hitam raksasa dalam cerita rakyat Katalan yang masuk ke rumah pada malam hari dan menindih dada orang tidur, menimbulkan sesak dan mimpi buruk.",
    "en": "The Pesanta is a huge black dog or cat of Catalan folklore that enters houses at night and sits on sleepers' chests, causing breathlessness and nightmares.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "pesanta-c01",
      "pesanta-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Pesanta adalah makhluk legenda Katalan, biasanya anjing raksasa dan jarang berupa kucing, yang pada malam hari masuk ke rumah dan menginjak dada orang tidur hingga sesak napas dan bermimpi buruk. Ia hitam dan berbulu, berkaki baja yang berlubang sehingga tidak dapat memungut apa pun. Begitu orang terbangun, ia kabur lewat celah jendela atau lubang kunci.",
      "en": "The Pesanta is a creature of Catalan legend, usually a huge dog and rarely a cat, that comes into houses at night and treads on sleepers' chests until they gasp and dream badly. It is black and hairy, with holed steel paws that can hold nothing. As soon as someone wakes, it escapes through a window gap or the keyhole.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pesanta-c01",
        "pesanta-c02",
        "pesanta-c03",
        "pesanta-c04",
        "pesanta-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada siang hari ia bersembunyi di gereja atau rumah kosong, dan di Garrotxa di kawah gunung api. Sosoknya mirip Pisadeira dari Portugal dan dikaitkan dengan kelumpuhan tidur.",
      "en": "By day it hides in churches or empty houses, and in the Garrotxa in volcanic craters. It resembles the Portuguese Pisadeira and is linked to sleep paralysis.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pesanta-c02",
        "pesanta-c05",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "pesanta-c01",
      "source_id": "pesanta-s1",
      "quote": "the Pesanta (Catalan pronunciation: [pəˈzantə]) is a creature Catalan legend and popular culture. It is generally in the shape of an enormous dog or cat and goes into people's houses in the night and steps on their chests, making it difficult for them to breathe and causing nightmares.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pesanta adalah makhluk legenda dan budaya populer Katalan, umumnya berwujud anjing atau kucing raksasa yang masuk ke rumah pada malam hari dan menginjak dada orang sehingga sulit bernapas dan bermimpi buruk.",
        "en": "The Pesanta is a creature of Catalan legend and popular culture, usually a huge dog or cat that enters houses at night and treads on people's chests, making it hard to breathe and causing nightmares."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pesanta-c02",
      "source_id": "pesanta-s1",
      "quote": "The Pesanta is similar to the Portuguese Pisadeira. It is black and hairy, with steel paws, but its paws have holes in them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pesanta mirip Pisadeira dari Portugal; ia hitam dan berbulu, berkaki baja, tetapi kakinya berlubang.",
        "en": "The Pesanta is like the Portuguese Pisadeira; it is black and hairy, with steel paws that have holes in them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pesanta-c03",
      "source_id": "pesanta-s2",
      "quote": "Su aspecto es peludo y negro, con patas de acero, pero agujereadas de tal forma que no puede coger nada de la tierra sin que se le caiga.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kakinya yang berlubang membuatnya tidak dapat memungut apa pun dari tanah tanpa terjatuh lagi.",
        "en": "Its holed paws mean it cannot pick anything up from the ground without dropping it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pesanta-c04",
      "source_id": "pesanta-s3",
      "quote": "així que algú es desperta, surt corrents pel forat més petit de la finestra o pel forat del pany i només s'és a temps de veure'n una petita ombra que fuig.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Begitu seseorang terbangun, ia lari lewat celah jendela terkecil atau lubang kunci, sehingga orang hanya sempat melihat bayangan kecil yang kabur.",
        "en": "As soon as someone wakes, it runs off through the smallest gap in the window or the keyhole, and one only glimpses a small fleeing shadow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pesanta-c05",
      "source_id": "pesanta-s3",
      "quote": "Es diu que la Pesanta s'amaga de dia en esglésies o cases abandonades i a la Garrotxa hom creu que s'amaga als cràters dels volcans.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon pada siang hari Pesanta bersembunyi di gereja atau rumah kosong, dan di Garrotxa orang percaya ia bersembunyi di kawah gunung api.",
        "en": "By day the Pesanta is said to hide in churches or abandoned houses, and in the Garrotxa people believe it hides in volcanic craters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pesanta-c06",
      "source_id": "pesanta-s3",
      "quote": "La figura de la Pesanta està relacionada amb una parasòmnia anomenada paràlisi del son.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sosok Pesanta dikaitkan dengan parasomnia yang disebut kelumpuhan tidur.",
        "en": "The figure of the Pesanta is linked to a parasomnia called sleep paralysis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pesanta-c07",
      "source_id": "pesanta-s2",
      "quote": "En la cultura popular catalana, la Pesanta es un animal mitológico con forma de perro enorme (raramente un gato)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol menyebutnya hewan mitologis berwujud anjing raksasa, jarang berupa kucing.",
        "en": "The Spanish article calls it a mythological animal shaped like a huge dog, rarely a cat."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Puigmal adalah raksasa salju mitologi Katalan, penjaga lembah Ribes yang membatu menjadi gunung, pelindung pohon dan hewan yang mengajari manusia membuat keju.",
    "en": "Puigmal is a snow giant of Catalan mythology, guardian of the Ribes valley turned to a mountain, protector of trees and animals who taught humans to make cheese.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "puigmal-c01",
      "puigmal-c02",
      "puigmal-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Puigmal adalah raksasa mitologi Katalan, \"tuan dan penguasa\" pegunungan lembah Ribes di Ripollès, yang kemudian membatu menjadi gunung bernama sama. Ia berpakaian salju dan melindungi pohon serta hewan dari serangan manusia, bila perlu dengan kekuatan.",
      "en": "Puigmal is a giant of Catalan mythology, 'lord and master' of the mountains of the Ribes valley in the Ripollès, later turned to stone as the mountain of that name. Dressed in snow, he protects trees and animals from human attack, by force if necessary.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "puigmal-c01",
        "puigmal-c02",
        "puigmal-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Suatu hari ia memerah rusa betina dan susunya menjadi keju di pakaian saljunya; keju itu ia berikan kepada seorang manusia dan akan terus tumbuh selama tidak dihabiskan, agar manusia tak perlu membunuh hewan. Ketika manusia itu kembali berburu, Puigmal menghukumnya mengejar buruan selamanya. Mitos raksasa salju Aneto mirip kisah ini.",
      "en": "One day he milked a doe and the milk became cheese on his snow clothes; he gave the cheese to a mortal, and it would keep growing as long as it was not finished, so humans need not kill animals. When the man returned to hunting, Puigmal condemned him to chase prey forever. The myth of the snow giant Aneto is similar.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "puigmal-c04",
        "puigmal-c05",
        "puigmal-c06",
        "puigmal-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "abilities": [],

      },
      "summary": {
        "id": "Puigmal mengubah susu rusa menjadi keju yang tak habis-habis bagi seorang manusia; ketika orang itu kembali berburu, ia dihukum mengejar buruan selamanya.",
        "en": "Puigmal turns a doe's milk into never-ending cheese for a mortal; when the man takes up hunting again, he is doomed to chase prey forever."

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "puigmal-c01",
      "source_id": "puigmal-s3",
      "quote": "Puigmal és el nom d'un gegant propi de la mitologia catalana. Era el guardià de les muntanyes de la vall de Ribes (tradicionalment se'n deia «amo i senyor») i quedà petrificat, en forma de muntanya (el Puigmal).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puigmal adalah raksasa dalam mitologi Katalan, penjaga pegunungan lembah Ribes (secara tradisional disebut \"tuan dan penguasa\") yang membatu menjadi gunung Puigmal.",
        "en": "Puigmal is a giant of Catalan mythology, guardian of the mountains of the Ribes valley (traditionally called 'lord and master'), who was turned to stone as the mountain Puigmal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puigmal-c02",
      "source_id": "puigmal-s3",
      "quote": "Se'l descriu sempre vestit de neu i se'l considera el protector d'arbres i animals, que defensa, si cal amb força, davant les agressions dels humans als regnes vegetal i animal.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia selalu digambarkan berpakaian salju dan dianggap pelindung pohon dan hewan, yang ia bela, bila perlu dengan kekuatan, dari serangan manusia.",
        "en": "He is always described as dressed in snow and is considered the protector of trees and animals, whom he defends, by force if need be, against human attacks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puigmal-c03",
      "source_id": "puigmal-s1",
      "quote": "In Catalan mythology, a Puigmal is considered the protector of trees and animals, who defends against attacks on humans. He is said to inhabit the mountains overlooking the valley of Ribes (Ripollès).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Inggris juga menyebutnya pelindung pohon dan hewan yang menghuni pegunungan di atas lembah Ribes (Ripollès).",
        "en": "The English article also calls him a protector of trees and animals living in the mountains above the valley of Ribes (Ripollès)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puigmal-c04",
      "source_id": "puigmal-s3",
      "quote": "S'explica que un dia va munyir una daina i en fregar-se les mans plenes de llet sobre el seu vestit de neu, la va convertir en formatge que va oferir a un mortal",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon suatu hari ia memerah susu seekor rusa betina, dan saat menggosokkan tangannya yang penuh susu ke pakaian saljunya, susu itu menjadi keju yang ia berikan kepada seorang manusia.",
        "en": "One day, it is told, he milked a doe, and rubbing his milky hands on his snow clothes turned the milk into cheese, which he gave to a mortal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puigmal-c05",
      "source_id": "puigmal-s2",
      "quote": "advirtiéndole que, mientras no se lo acabara todo, el queso volvería a crecer y los humanos podrían alimentarse toda la vida sin matar animales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berpesan bahwa selama keju itu tidak dihabiskan, keju itu akan tumbuh lagi dan manusia dapat makan seumur hidup tanpa membunuh hewan.",
        "en": "He warned that as long as it was not all eaten, the cheese would grow back and humans could feed on it all their lives without killing animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puigmal-c06",
      "source_id": "puigmal-s3",
      "quote": "Quan, més tard, aquell home va tornar als vells costums i a caçar animals per a subsistir, Puigmal el va condemnar a perseguir eternament les seves preses, que mai no pot atènyer, en una mena d'interpretació del mite del Mal Caçador.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika manusia itu kembali berburu, Puigmal menghukumnya untuk selamanya mengejar buruan yang tak pernah tertangkap, semacam tafsir mitos Pemburu Jahat.",
        "en": "When the man later went back to hunting, Puigmal condemned him to chase forever prey he can never catch, a kind of version of the Wild Hunter myth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "puigmal-c07",
      "source_id": "puigmal-s2",
      "quote": "Sobre enseñar que es el queso a los humanos hay un mito parecido, el gigante de nieve llamado Aneto",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada mitos serupa tentang raksasa salju Aneto yang mengajari manusia membuat keju.",
        "en": "There is a similar myth of the snow giant Aneto teaching humans about cheese."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, es, dan ca; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."

    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ramidreju adalah hewan mitos Cantabria mirip musang bertubuh panjang seperti ular, lahir sekali tiap seratus tahun, yang bulunya menyembuhkan segala penyakit.",
    "en": "The ramidreju is a Cantabrian mythical weasel-like animal with a snake-long body, born once a century, whose fur cures every illness.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ramidreju-c01",
      "ramidreju-c02",
      "ramidreju-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ramidreju adalah makhluk mitologi Cantabria yang hidup di gunung dan hutan Spanyol utara. Hewan mirip musang ini lahir sekali setiap seratus tahun dari musang atau marten; tubuhnya panjang seperti ular, bulunya kehijauan, matanya kuning, dan dengan taring seperti babi hutan ia menggali lubang yang sangat dalam.",
      "en": "The ramidreju is a creature of Cantabrian mythology living in the mountains and forests of northern Spain. This weasel-like animal is born once every hundred years from a weasel or marten; it has a long snake-like body, greenish fur and yellow eyes, and digs very deep holes with boar-like tusks.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ramidreju-c01",
        "ramidreju-c02",
        "ramidreju-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia sangat diburu karena bulunya menyembuhkan segala penyakit dan ia dapat membantu menemukan harta; menurut Manuel Llano, yang mendengarnya dari warga Viaña, empedunya diminum para anjana di lembah Cieza.",
      "en": "It is much hunted because its fur cures all ills and it can lead to treasure; according to Manuel Llano, who heard of it from a resident of Viaña, the anjanas of the Cieza valley drink its bile.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ramidreju-c03",

      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ramidreju-c01",
      "source_id": "ramidreju-s1",
      "quote": "In Cantabrian mythology, a ramidreju (Cantabrian: [ramiˈdrehu]) is a creature said to inhabit the mountains and forests of Cantabria in northern Spain. This animal, which resembles a weasel, is born once every hundred years from a weasel or a marten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Cantabria, ramidreju adalah makhluk yang konon menghuni gunung dan hutan Cantabria di Spanyol utara; hewan mirip musang ini lahir sekali setiap seratus tahun dari musang atau marten.",
        "en": "In Cantabrian mythology the ramidreju is a creature said to live in the mountains and forests of Cantabria in northern Spain; this weasel-like animal is born once every hundred years from a weasel or a marten."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ramidreju-c02",
      "source_id": "ramidreju-s1",
      "quote": "These mythological creatures have a very long body, like a snake, and their fur is slightly green-colored. Its eyes are yellow, its nose is like that of a hog and its tusks are like those of the boar, which it uses to dig very deep holes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tubuhnya sangat panjang seperti ular dengan bulu kehijauan, matanya kuning, hidungnya seperti babi, dan taringnya seperti babi hutan yang dipakai menggali lubang sangat dalam.",
        "en": "Its body is very long like a snake's, its fur greenish, its eyes yellow, its nose like a hog's and its tusks like a boar's, which it uses to dig very deep holes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ramidreju-c03",
      "source_id": "ramidreju-s1",
      "quote": "Ramidrejus are a very sought-after animal in Cantabrian folklore because their fur heals every sickness and the animal has a strong desire for gold.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ramidreju sangat diburu dalam cerita rakyat Cantabria karena bulunya menyembuhkan segala penyakit dan hewan ini sangat menginginkan emas.",
        "en": "Ramidrejus are much sought after in Cantabrian folklore because their fur cures every illness and the animal has a strong craving for gold."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ramidreju-c04",
      "source_id": "ramidreju-s2",
      "quote": "Fue descrito por el cantabrista Manuel Llano, según un relato en el que dice haber oído a un vecino de Viaña (Cabuérniga) hablar de él.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ramidreju dideskripsikan oleh peneliti Cantabria Manuel Llano berdasarkan cerita yang katanya ia dengar dari seorang warga Viaña (Cabuérniga).",
        "en": "It was described by the Cantabrian scholar Manuel Llano, from an account he said he heard from a resident of Viaña (Cabuérniga)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ramidreju-c05",
      "source_id": "ramidreju-s2",
      "quote": "su hiel -que es bebida por las anjanas en el valle de Cieza- posee la capacidad de curar todas las enfermedades y desvelar tesoros ocultos.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Empedunya, yang diminum para anjana di lembah Cieza, dapat menyembuhkan segala penyakit dan mengungkap harta tersembunyi.",
        "en": "Its bile, drunk by the anjanas in the Cieza valley, can cure all illnesses and reveal hidden treasures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ramidreju-c06",
      "source_id": "ramidreju-s2",
      "quote": "descrito como híbrido de la monuca galana (comadreja) y de la rámila (garduña), delgado y largo, con piel rayada de negro y verde, y con cara de jabalí y ojos amarillos.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol menggambarkannya sebagai hibrida monuca galana (musang) dan rámila (marten batu), ramping dan panjang, berkulit belang hitam-hijau, berwajah babi hutan, dan bermata kuning.",
        "en": "The Spanish article describes it as a hybrid of the monuca galana (weasel) and the rámila (stone marten), slender and long, with black-and-green striped skin, a boar's face and yellow eyes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ramidreju-c07",
      "source_id": "ramidreju-s3",
      "quote": "Oni tre volas ilin ĉar per ili oni povas trovi kaŝitajn trezorojn kaj ĉar onidire ties felo kuracas ĉiun malsanon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Esperanto juga menyebut ramidreju sangat dicari karena dapat membantu menemukan harta tersembunyi dan bulunya konon menyembuhkan segala penyakit.",
        "en": "The Esperanto article also says ramidrejus are much sought because they help find hidden treasure and their fur reputedly cures every disease."
      }
    }
  ],

  "schema": "mythics-entry/1",

  "culture": {

  },
  "region": {

    "claim_ids": [
      "scrofa-semilanuta-c01"
    ]
  },
  "countries": null,
  "era": null,
  "habitats": [],
  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Scrofa semilanuta adalah babi betina berbulu domba pada separuh depan tubuhnya, makhluk dalam legenda pendirian Milan dan lambang kuno kota itu.",
    "en": "The scrofa semilanuta is a sow woolly on the front half of its body, a creature of Milan's founding legend and the city's ancient emblem.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "scrofa-semilanuta-c01",
      "scrofa-semilanuta-c02",
      "scrofa-semilanuta-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Scrofa semilanuta, \"babi setengah berbulu domba\", adalah makhluk legendaris dan lambang kuno Milan. Menurut legenda yang sebagian bersumber dari Livius, pangeran Galia Belloveso tiba di Lembah Po setelah seorang dewi menunjukkan dalam mimpi tempat kota itu akan berdiri; di sana ia melihat babi betina berbulu domba panjang pada separuh depan tubuhnya. Versi Esperanto menyebut kota itu lalu dinamai Mediolanum.",
      "en": "The scrofa semilanuta, 'half-woollen sow', is a legendary creature and the ancient emblem of Milan. By a legend partly drawn from Livy, the Gaulish prince Belloveso came to the Po Valley after a goddess showed him in a dream where the city would rise; there he saw a sow with long wool on the front half of its body. The Esperanto version says the city was then named Mediolanum.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "scrofa-semilanuta-c01",
        "scrofa-semilanuta-c02",
        "scrofa-semilanuta-c03",
        "scrofa-semilanuta-c04",
        "scrofa-semilanuta-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penulis kuno seperti Sidonius Apollinaris, Datius, dan Alciato menghubungkannya dengan nama Mediolanum, tafsiran yang kini ditolak para sarjana; versi lain menyebutnya Khimaira setengah babi hutan setengah domba. Claudianus menyebutnya dalam sebuah epitalamium. Relief di Palazzo della Ragione dari masa pembangunan gedung itu (1228–1233) menjadi kunci perdebatan, dan lambang ini dipakai sampai munculnya biscione Visconti.",
      "en": "Ancient writers such as Sidonius Apollinaris, Datius and Alciato tied it to the name Mediolanum, a reading scholars now reject; another version calls it a Chimera, half boar and half ram. Claudian mentions it in an epithalamium. A relief on the Palazzo della Ragione from the building's construction (1228–1233) is central to the debate, and the emblem lasted until the Visconti biscione.",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c01",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s1",
      "quote": "The scrofa semilanuta (English: \"half-woollen boar\") is an ancient emblem of the city of Milan, Italy, dating back at least to the Middle Ages — and, according to a local legend, to the very foundation of Milan.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Scrofa semilanuta (\"babi setengah berbulu domba\") adalah lambang kuno kota Milan, Italia, yang berasal setidaknya dari Abad Pertengahan dan, menurut legenda setempat, dari saat kota itu didirikan.",
        "en": "The scrofa semilanuta ('half-woollen boar') is an ancient emblem of the city of Milan, Italy, dating at least from the Middle Ages and, by local legend, from the city's very foundation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c02",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s2",
      "quote": "La scrofa semilanuta è una creatura leggendaria, simbolo della città di Milano prima dell'età comunale, che si riallaccia alla fondazione del capoluogo lombardo, avvenuta ad opera dei Celti.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Scrofa semilanuta adalah makhluk legendaris, lambang Milan sebelum masa komune, yang terkait dengan pendirian ibu kota Lombardia itu oleh bangsa Kelt.",
        "en": "The scrofa semilanuta is a legendary creature, the symbol of Milan before the communal age, tied to the founding of the Lombard capital by the Celts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c03",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s1",
      "quote": "According to this legend (which partially draws from Livy's writings), the founder of Milan was a Gaul prince named Belloveso. Belloveso reached the Po Valley following a vision he had had in a dream, where a goddess showed him the place where the city would rise.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda yang sebagian bersumber dari tulisan Livius, pendiri Milan adalah pangeran Galia bernama Belloveso, yang tiba di Lembah Po setelah dalam mimpi seorang dewi menunjukkan tempat kota itu akan berdiri.",
        "en": "By this legend, partly drawn from Livy, Milan's founder was a Gaulish prince named Belloveso, who reached the Po Valley after a goddess showed him in a dream where the city would rise."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c04",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s1",
      "quote": "In this dream, he saw a sow with unusually long wool on the front half of its body.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mimpi itu ia melihat babi betina berbulu domba sangat panjang pada separuh depan tubuhnya.",
        "en": "In the dream he saw a sow with unusually long wool on the front half of its body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c05",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s3",
      "quote": "La kelta estro decidis tie konstrui sian urbon kaj nomigi ĝin Mediolanum, tio estas \"semilanuta\" (medio-lanum, duonlaneca).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Esperanto, pemimpin Kelt itu membangun kotanya di sana dan menamainya Mediolanum, yakni \"semilanuta\" (medio-lanum, setengah berbulu domba).",
        "en": "According to the Esperanto article, the Celtic chief built his city there and named it Mediolanum, that is 'semilanuta' (medio-lanum, half-woollen)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c06",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s1",
      "quote": "Several ancient sources (including Sidonius Apollinaris, Datius, and, more recently, Andrea Alciato) have argued that the scrofa semilanuta is connected to the etymology of the ancient name of Milan, \"Mediolanum\", and this is still occasionally mentioned in modern sources, although this interpretation has long been dismissed by scholars.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa sumber kuno, termasuk Sidonius Apollinaris, Datius, dan kemudian Andrea Alciato, mengaitkan scrofa semilanuta dengan asal nama kuno Milan, Mediolanum, tetapi tafsiran ini sudah lama ditolak para sarjana.",
        "en": "Several ancient sources, including Sidonius Apollinaris, Datius and later Andrea Alciato, linked the scrofa semilanuta to the origin of Milan's ancient name, Mediolanum, but scholars have long dismissed this."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c07",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s1",
      "quote": "Other ancient sources (most notably the aforementioned Alciato, who in turns credits Ambrose for his account) report that the half-woolly sow is actually a sort of Chimera — half boar and half ram — and that the emblem came about when the Bituriges and the Aedui, having as their emblems a ram and a boar respectively, joined in the Po Valley.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber kuno lain, terutama Alciato yang merujuk Ambrosius, menyebutnya semacam Khimaira setengah babi hutan setengah domba jantan, lambang yang lahir saat kaum Bituriges (lambang domba) dan Aedui (lambang babi hutan) bersatu di Lembah Po.",
        "en": "Other ancient sources, notably Alciato citing Ambrose, call it a sort of Chimera, half boar and half ram, an emblem born when the Bituriges (ram) and the Aedui (boar) joined in the Po Valley."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c08",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s2",
      "quote": "Claudio Claudiano la citò nell'Epithalamium de nuptiis Honorii Augusti.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Claudianus menyebutnya dalam Epithalamium de nuptiis Honorii Augusti.",
        "en": "Claudian mentioned it in the Epithalamium de nuptiis Honorii Augusti."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c09",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s1",
      "quote": "A key element of this controversy is a bas relief affixed to the walls of the Palazzo della Ragione, former broletto (administrative building) of the medieval commune of Milan. The bas relief is reportedly a medieval copy of an older one, found during the excavations when the Palazzo was built (1228–1233).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kunci perdebatannya adalah relief di dinding Palazzo della Ragione, gedung pemerintahan komune Milan abad pertengahan, yang konon salinan relief lebih tua yang ditemukan saat gedung itu dibangun (1228–1233).",
        "en": "Key to the debate is a bas relief on the wall of the Palazzo della Ragione, the medieval commune's administrative building, reportedly a copy of an older relief found while the Palazzo was built (1228–1233)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "scrofa-semilanuta-c10",
      "source_id": "scrofa-semilanuta-s1",
      "quote": "In any case, the \"scrofa semilanuta\" was thereafter adopted as the main emblem of Milan, at least until the advent of the biscione of the House of Visconti. Another (more recent) representation of the \"scrofa semilanuta\" is found in the internal courtyard of Palazzo Marino (Milan's city hall).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak itu scrofa semilanuta menjadi lambang utama Milan setidaknya sampai munculnya biscione Wangsa Visconti; gambaran yang lebih baru ada di halaman dalam Palazzo Marino, balai kota Milan.",
        "en": "Thereafter the scrofa semilanuta was Milan's main emblem at least until the Visconti biscione arrived; a more recent depiction is in the inner courtyard of Palazzo Marino, Milan's city hall."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Šemík adalah kuda putih legendaris Ceko yang dapat berbicara, tunggangan Horymír, yang menyelamatkan tuannya dengan melompat dari tebing Vyšehrad lalu mati karena lukanya.",
    "en": "Šemík is a legendary talking white horse of Czech lore, Horymír's mount, who saved his master by leaping from the Vyšehrad rock and then died of his injuries.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "semik-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Šemík adalah kuda putih legendaris dalam mitologi Slavia Ceko, cerdas dan dapat berbicara, milik bangsawan kecil Horymír dari Neumětely. Horymír menentang Pangeran Křesomysl yang mendorong rakyat meninggalkan ladang demi tambang. Ketika para penambang membakar rumahnya, ia membalas, lalu Šemík membawanya secara gaib melintasi angin ke Vyšehrad sebelum fajar.",
      "en": "Šemík is a legendary white horse of the Slavic mythology of Czechia, clever and able to speak, owned by the squire Horymír of Neumětely. Horymír opposed Prince Křesomysl, who urged people to leave the fields for the mines. When the miners burned his home he took revenge, and Šemík magically bore him through the wind to Vyšehrad before dawn.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "semik-c01",
        "semik-c02",
        "semik-c03",
        "semik-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dijatuhi hukuman mati, Horymír meminta berkeliling halaman kastel di atas kudanya; Šemík lalu melompat dari tebing Vyšehrad, membawanya pulang ke Neumětely, dan mati karena lukanya. Jiwanya konon tidur di batu karang Vyšehrad menunggu Bohemia membutuhkannya. Makamnya di Neumětely dilindungi sejak 1887. Legenda ini pertama muncul dalam kronik Václav Hájek z Libočan dan dipopulerkan Alois Jirásek.",
      "en": "Sentenced to death, Horymír asked to ride round the castle courtyard; Šemík then leapt from the Vyšehrad rock, carried him home to Neumětely, and died of his wounds. His soul is said to sleep in the Vyšehrad rock until Bohemia needs him. His tomb at Neumětely has been honoured since 1887. The legend first appears in the chronicle of Václav Hájek z Libočan and was popularised by Alois Jirásek.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "semik-c01",
      "source_id": "semik-s1",
      "quote": "Šemík est, dans la mythologie slave de Tchéquie, un cheval blanc légendaire doué de parole et d'une grande intelligence, monture du personnage légendaire Horymír.",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia Ceko, Šemík adalah kuda putih legendaris yang dapat berbicara dan sangat cerdas, tunggangan tokoh legenda Horymír.",
        "en": "In the Slavic mythology of Czechia, Šemík is a legendary white horse gifted with speech and great intelligence, the mount of the legendary Horymír."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c02",
      "source_id": "semik-s1",
      "quote": "Horymír était un hobereau qui vivait dans le village de Neumětely, pendant le règne de Křesomysl.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Horymír adalah bangsawan kecil yang tinggal di desa Neumětely pada masa pemerintahan Křesomysl.",
        "en": "Horymír was a squire living in the village of Neumětely in the reign of Křesomysl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c03",
      "source_id": "semik-s1",
      "quote": "Le prince avait une obsession pour les richesses, et en automne plusieurs hobereaux, avec Horymír à leur tête, allèrent voir Křesomysl pour le mettre en garde qu'une famine arriverait bientôt s'il continuait à encourager son peuple à délaisser les champs et pâturages au profit des mines",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sang pangeran terobsesi kekayaan; para bangsawan kecil dipimpin Horymír memperingatkan Křesomysl bahwa kelaparan akan datang bila rakyat terus didorong meninggalkan ladang dan padang rumput demi tambang.",
        "en": "The prince was obsessed with wealth; squires led by Horymír warned Křesomysl that famine would come if he kept urging his people to leave fields and pastures for the mines."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c04",
      "source_id": "semik-s1",
      "quote": "Šemík transporta par magie Horymír à travers le vent pour arriver en un instant devant la résidence du vévoda de Vyšehrad, au même moment, l'aube se leva.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah Horymír membalas dendam kepada para penambang, Šemík secara gaib membawanya melintasi angin sehingga dalam sekejap tiba di Vyšehrad tepat saat fajar.",
        "en": "After Horymír took revenge on the miners, Šemík magically carried him through the wind to arrive in an instant at Vyšehrad, just as dawn broke."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c05",
      "source_id": "semik-s1",
      "quote": "Horymír demanda pour dernière volonté de faire le tour de la cour du château de Vyšehrad sur le dos de son cheval.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat dijatuhi hukuman mati, permintaan terakhir Horymír adalah berkeliling halaman kastel Vyšehrad di atas punggung kudanya.",
        "en": "Condemned to death, Horymír's last wish was to ride round the courtyard of Vyšehrad castle on his horse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c06",
      "source_id": "semik-s3",
      "quote": "Jeho pánem byl Horymír, vladyka neumětelský, kterého Šemík zachránil před jistou smrtí odvážným skokem ze skály u Vyšehradu. Poté Horymíra dovezl do Neumětel a podlehl zraněním, která si způsobil skokem.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Ceko, Šemík menyelamatkan tuannya Horymír dari kematian dengan melompat berani dari tebing Vyšehrad, membawanya pulang ke Neumětely, lalu mati karena luka akibat lompatan itu.",
        "en": "According to the Czech article, Šemík saved his master Horymír from certain death with a daring leap from the Vyšehrad rock, carried him home to Neumětely and then died of the injuries from the jump."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c07",
      "source_id": "semik-s1",
      "quote": "La légende veut que l'âme de Šemík dorme dans le rocher de Vyšehrad en attendant le moment où la Bohême aura de nouveau besoin de lui.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda menyebut jiwa Šemík tidur di dalam batu karang Vyšehrad, menunggu saat Bohemia kembali membutuhkannya.",
        "en": "Legend says Šemík's soul sleeps in the Vyšehrad rock, waiting until Bohemia needs him again."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c08",
      "source_id": "semik-s1",
      "quote": "Une tombe fut érigée en 1887 en l'honneur de ce cheval, financée à l'aide d'or donné par Charles Joseph Adolphe III de Schwarzenberg (1824-1904)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1887 didirikan makam untuk menghormati kuda ini, dibiayai emas pemberian Charles Joseph Adolphe III dari Schwarzenberg (1824-1904).",
        "en": "A tomb was raised in 1887 in the horse's honour, paid for with gold given by Charles Joseph Adolphe III of Schwarzenberg (1824-1904)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c09",
      "source_id": "semik-s2",
      "quote": "Horymír i jego koń Šemík nie występują w źródłach historycznych. Legenda po raz pierwszy pojawia się w Kronice czeskiej Václava Hájka z Libočan. Temat opracował literacko głównie Alois Jirásek w książce Staré pověsti české.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Horymír dan kudanya tidak muncul dalam sumber sejarah; legenda ini pertama kali muncul dalam Kronika Ceko karya Václav Hájek z Libočan dan digarap secara sastra terutama oleh Alois Jirásek dalam Staré pověsti české.",
        "en": "Horymír and his horse do not appear in historical sources; the legend first appears in the Czech Chronicle of Václav Hájek z Libočan and was retold mainly by Alois Jirásek in Staré pověsti české."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "semik-c10",
      "source_id": "semik-s2",
      "quote": "Legenda opowiada o rywalizacji między dążeniem do wydobycia złota i srebra oraz pogonią za pieniądzem, które reprezentuje książę Krzesomysł, a pracą na roli reprezentowaną przez Horymíra.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Polandia, legenda ini mengisahkan persaingan antara pengejaran emas, perak, dan uang yang diwakili Pangeran Křesomysl dengan kerja bertani yang diwakili Horymír.",
        "en": "According to the Polish article, the legend tells of a contest between the pursuit of gold, silver and money, embodied by Prince Křesomysl, and farming, embodied by Horymír."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tamarro adalah hewan khayalan Pyrenees Katalan yang tak pernah tertangkap, dipakai dalam perburuan malam pura-pura untuk mengerjai pendatang, mirip dahu.",
    "en": "The tamarro is an imaginary Catalan Pyrenean animal that is never caught, used in mock night hunts to fool newcomers, much like the dahu.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tamarro-c01",
      "tamarro-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tamarro adalah hewan khayalan dalam mitologi Pyrenees Katalan, juga dikenal sebagai tamarre di Aubrac dan Aveyron. Menurut legenda ia licin dan selalu bersembunyi di lembah, sehingga hampir mustahil diburu. Wujudnya tak pernah digambarkan pasti; umumnya ia padanan dahu, yang kakinya di satu sisi lebih pendek.",
      "en": "The tamarro is an imaginary animal of Catalan Pyrenean mythology, also known as tamarre in Aubrac and Aveyron. By legend it is elusive and stays hidden in the valleys, so it can hardly be hunted. Its form is never fixed; it is mostly the counterpart of the dahu, with legs shorter on one side.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tamarro-c01",
        "tamarro-c02",
        "tamarro-c03",
        "tamarro-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Perburuan tamarro pada malam hari, dengan tongkat, batu, dan karung, adalah lelucon untuk mengerjai pendatang lugu sekaligus ritual inisiasi; di Andorra korbannya diberi tahu bahwa kulit tamarro sangat berharga. Gambutzí dan gamusino adalah makhluk serupa. Tamarro menjadi maskot pesta olahraga di Andorra la Vella (1991), nama dinosaurus Tamarro insperatus (2021), dan hewan totem Vendargues.",
      "en": "The night tamarro hunt, with sticks, stones and sacks, is a prank on naive newcomers and also a rite of initiation; in Andorra the victims were told its pelt was precious. The gambutzí and gamusino are similar creatures. The tamarro became the mascot of games in Andorra la Vella (1991), the name of the dinosaur Tamarro insperatus (2021), and a totem animal of Vendargues.",
