  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c01",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "Lou Drapé (lo drapet in the classical norm of Occitan, lou or lo meaning \"the\") is a legendary folkloric horse of the town of Aigues-Mortes in the Gard region, in the Petite Camargue marsh area of France.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lou Drapé (lo drapet dalam ejaan Oksitan baku) adalah kuda legendaris dari cerita rakyat kota Aigues-Mortes di Gard, di daerah rawa Petite Camargue, Prancis.",
        "en": "Lou Drapé (lo drapet in standard Occitan) is a legendary folk horse of the town of Aigues-Mortes in the Gard, in the Petite Camargue marshes of France."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c02",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "It is said to wander around the walls of the city at night and to take a large number of children on his back to abduct them. These children never return from this journey.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon ia berkeliaran di sekitar tembok kota pada malam hari dan menculik banyak anak di punggungnya, dan anak-anak itu tidak pernah kembali.",
        "en": "It is said to roam the town walls at night and carry off many children on its back, and they never come back."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c03",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "Lou Drapé could be a version of the drac legend of Occitania, an evil creature that can assume the shape of a horse. The image of this ghastly horse is a symbol of death. It is used to scare children, like the bogeyman or the big bad wolf in other regions of France.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lou Drapé mungkin versi legenda drac Occitania, makhluk jahat yang dapat berwujud kuda; kuda pucat ini lambang kematian dan dipakai menakuti anak seperti momok atau serigala jahat di daerah lain Prancis.",
        "en": "Lou Drapé may be a version of the Occitan drac, an evil creature that can take horse shape; this ghastly horse is a symbol of death, used to scare children like the bogeyman or big bad wolf elsewhere in France."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c04",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "According to Frédéric Mistral, drapet or draquet is the name given to a small drac, a small lutin in the Languedoc region.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Frédéric Mistral, drapet atau draquet adalah sebutan untuk drac kecil, sejenis peri usil kecil di Languedoc.",
        "en": "According to Frédéric Mistral, drapet or draquet is the name of a small drac, a little sprite of Languedoc."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c05",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "However, there exists no direct source to explain the name \"lou Drapé\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namun tidak ada sumber langsung yang menjelaskan nama Lou Drapé.",
        "en": "However, no direct source explains the name Lou Drapé."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c06",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "“Lou Drapé\" is mentioned in an 1818 text by Jacques-Albin-Simon Collin de Plancy in his book on demonic creatures, the Dictionnaire infernal",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lou Drapé disebut dalam teks tahun 1818 karya Jacques-Albin-Simon Collin de Plancy, Dictionnaire infernal.",
        "en": "Lou Drapé is mentioned in an 1818 text by Jacques-Albin-Simon Collin de Plancy, the Dictionnaire infernal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c07",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that when lou Drapé is out, it gathers all the lost children on its back, one after the other; and that its rump gradually lengthens from a standard size to one long enough to fit fifty and a hundred children, which he then takes who knows where.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Collin de Plancy, saat keluar Lou Drapé mengumpulkan semua anak yang tersesat di punggungnya satu per satu, dan pantatnya memanjang hingga muat lima puluh sampai seratus anak, yang dibawanya entah ke mana.",
        "en": "According to Collin de Plancy, when out, Lou Drapé gathers all the lost children on its back one by one, its rump lengthening until it holds fifty or a hundred children, whom it takes who knows where."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c08",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "The Guide de la Provence mystérieuse, published in 1965, says that children at the time were still being threatened with the coming of lou Drapé.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guide de la Provence mystérieuse (1965) menyebut anak-anak pada masa itu masih ditakut-takuti dengan kedatangan Lou Drapé.",
        "en": "The Guide de la Provence mystérieuse (1965) says children were still being threatened with Lou Drapé at that time."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c09",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s1",
      "quote": "The last child to have ridden the horse is said to have freed all the others by screaming \"Jesus, Mary, great Saint Joseph!!\" and jumping to the ground, which would cause lou Drapé to disappear.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anak terakhir yang menungganginya konon membebaskan semua anak lain dengan berteriak \"Yesus, Maria, Santo Yosef yang agung!\" lalu melompat ke tanah, sehingga Lou Drapé lenyap.",
        "en": "The last child to ride it is said to have freed all the others by crying 'Jesus, Mary, great Saint Joseph!' and leaping to the ground, making Lou Drapé vanish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c10",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s2",
      "quote": "Il rejoint un folklore abondant de chevaux maléfiques et ravisseurs, souvent en relation avec l'élément liquide.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Prancis menyebut Lou Drapé bagian dari banyak cerita rakyat tentang kuda jahat penculik yang sering terkait dengan air.",
        "en": "The French article says Lou Drapé belongs to a rich folklore of evil kidnapping horses, often linked with water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drape-legend-c11",
      "source_id": "drape-legend-s3",
      "quote": "entre els quals hi ha el drac pirinenc, una criatura que pot prendre també la forma de cavall o ase i carrega nens a l'esquena per a ofegar-los al riu.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Katalan menyebut salah satunya drac Pyrenees, yang dapat berwujud kuda atau keledai dan membawa anak di punggungnya untuk menenggelamkan mereka di sungai.",
        "en": "The Catalan article names among them the Pyrenean drac, which can take the form of a horse or donkey and carries children on its back to drown them in the river."
      }
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ethiopian-pegasus-c01",
      "source_id": "ethiopian-pegasus-s1",
      "quote": "An Ethiopian pegasus is an animal from medieval bestiaries. According to Pliny the Elder, they were a breed of two-horned, winged horses from Ethiopia. They reportedly lived on an island in the Red Sea off the coast of Eritrea.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pegasus Etiopia adalah hewan dari bestiari abad pertengahan; menurut Plinius Tua, mereka ras kuda bersayap bertanduk dua dari Etiopia yang konon hidup di sebuah pulau di Laut Merah lepas pantai Eritrea.",
        "en": "The Ethiopian pegasus is an animal of medieval bestiaries; according to Pliny the Elder they were a breed of two-horned winged horses from Ethiopia, said to live on an island in the Red Sea off Eritrea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ethiopian-pegasus-c02",
      "source_id": "ethiopian-pegasus-s1",
      "quote": "In antiquity and the Middle Ages, authors used \"Aithiopia\"/“Ethiopia” broadly, often meaning areas south of Egypt where people had darker skin then the Greeks.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada zaman kuno dan Abad Pertengahan, penulis memakai nama Aithiopia atau Ethiopia secara luas, sering untuk wilayah di selatan Mesir.",
        "en": "In antiquity and the Middle Ages, writers used 'Aithiopia' or 'Ethiopia' broadly, often for areas south of Egypt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ethiopian-pegasus-c03",
      "source_id": "ethiopian-pegasus-s2",
      "quote": "Les chevaux ailés d'Éthiopie ou pégases d'Éthiopie sont mentionnés par Pline l'Ancien puis repris par certains bestiaires médiévaux.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, kuda bersayap Etiopia disebut Plinius Tua lalu diambil alih sejumlah bestiari abad pertengahan.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the winged horses of Ethiopia are mentioned by Pliny the Elder and then taken up by some medieval bestiaries."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ethiopian-pegasus-c04",
      "source_id": "ethiopian-pegasus-s3",
      "quote": "«В Ефіопії народжуються... багато чудовиськ — [зокрема] крилаті коні, озброєні рогами, звані пегасами».",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Ukraina mengutip Naturalis Historia karya Plinius: \"Di Etiopia lahir banyak monster, di antaranya kuda bersayap bersenjata tanduk yang disebut pegasus.\"",
        "en": "The Ukrainian article quotes Pliny's Natural History: 'In Ethiopia are born many monsters, among them winged horses armed with horns, called pegasi.'"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ethiopian-pegasus-c05",
      "source_id": "ethiopian-pegasus-s3",
      "quote": "Тварина із середньовічних бестіаріїв, таких як «Der Naturen Bloeme» (бл. 1350 р.), де він постає як крилатий кінь з рогами.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul dalam bestiari abad pertengahan seperti Der Naturen Bloeme (sekitar 1350) sebagai kuda bersayap bertanduk.",
        "en": "It appears in medieval bestiaries such as Der Naturen Bloeme (c. 1350) as a horned winged horse."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Etsai adalah iblis mitologi Basque yang menjalankan sekolah di gua, mengajarkan segala ilmu, tetapi menahan satu murid selamanya sebagai bayaran.",
    "en": "Etsai is a devil of Basque mythology who runs a school in a cave, teaching all knowledge but keeping one pupil forever as payment.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "etsai-c01",
      "etsai-c07",
      "etsai-c09"
    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Etsai, \"iblis, musuh\" dalam bahasa Basque, juga disebut Deabru, Galtzagorri, dan banyak nama lain, adalah iblis dan musuh agama yang ingin mencelakai manusia, tetapi dapat dikalahkan dengan kecerdikan. Ia muncul pada malam hari sebagai naga, manusia, atau hewan seperti banteng, kuda, babi, dan kambing.",
      "en": "Etsai, Basque for 'devil, enemy', also called Deabru, Galtzagorri and many other names, is the devil and an enemy of religion who seeks to harm people but can be outwitted. He appears at night as a dragon, a man or an animal such as a bull, horse, pig or goat.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "etsai-c02",
        "etsai-c03",
        "etsai-c04",
        "etsai-c05",
        "etsai-c06"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Ia menjalankan sekolah di gua Leiza di Sare atau Akelarren-leze di Zugarramurdi yang menyaingi universitas Salamanca; Axular konon belajar di sana. Sebagai bayaran, satu murid harus tinggal selamanya sebagai tawanannya. Dalam versi Ordizia, Atarrabi dan Mikelats belajar di sana, dan Atarrabi menggantikan adiknya yang kalah undian.",
      "en": "He ran a school in the Leiza cave at Sare or Akelarren-leze at Zugarramurdi that rivalled the university of Salamanca; Axular is said to have studied there.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "etsai-c01",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Etsai (prononcer « etchaye») est un des diables dans la mythologie basque qui exerce la fonction de professeur dans des grottes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Etsai adalah salah satu iblis mitologi Basque yang berperan sebagai guru di gua-gua.",
        "en": "Etsai is one of the devils of Basque mythology, who works as a teacher in caves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c02",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Etsai signifie « diable, ennemi » en basque.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Etsai berarti \"iblis, musuh\" dalam bahasa Basque.",
        "en": "Etsai means 'devil, enemy' in Basque."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c03",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Il possède de nombreux noms, Deabru « diable », Gaizkiñ « malfaisant », Galtzagorri « pantalon rouge », Kapagorri « cape rouge », Gorritxiki « petit rouge »",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia punya banyak nama: Deabru (\"iblis\"), Gaizkiñ (\"jahat\"), Galtzagorri (\"celana merah\"), Kapagorri (\"jubah merah\"), dan Gorritxiki (\"si merah kecil\").",
        "en": "He has many names: Deabru ('devil'), Gaizkiñ ('evil-doer'), Galtzagorri ('red trousers'), Kapagorri ('red cape') and Gorritxiki ('little red')."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c04",
      "source_id": "etsai-s2",
      "quote": "Etsai és el diable, un dimoni, un esperit maligne i un enemic de la religió. El seu objectiu és fer mal als homes, o fer-los fer mal, però no és invencible i és possible escapar de la seva influència amb una mica d'astúcia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Katalan, Etsai adalah iblis, roh jahat, dan musuh agama yang berusaha mencelakai manusia atau membuat mereka berbuat jahat, tetapi ia tidak tak terkalahkan dan pengaruhnya dapat dihindari dengan sedikit kecerdikan.",
        "en": "According to the Catalan article, Etsai is the devil, an evil spirit and an enemy of religion who aims to harm people or make them do harm, but he is not invincible and his influence can be escaped with a little cunning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c05",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Il a tendance à prendre plusieurs formes, selon la légende: Il a souvent la forme d'un dragon ou d'un humain, mais il est aussi souvent représenté avec le corps d'un animal.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengambil berbagai wujud: sering naga atau manusia, tetapi juga bertubuh hewan.",
        "en": "He takes many forms: often a dragon or a human, but also an animal body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c06",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "On croit que ce génie apparaît de nuit, parfois sous l'aspect d'un taureau (zezen en basque), d'un cheval (zaldi), d'un porc (txerri) ou d'une chèvre (ahuntz).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh ini dipercaya muncul pada malam hari, kadang sebagai banteng (zezen), kuda (zaldi), babi (txerri), atau kambing (ahuntz).",
        "en": "This spirit is believed to appear at night, sometimes as a bull (zezen), horse (zaldi), pig (txerri) or goat (ahuntz)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c07",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Il habitait la grotte de Leiza à Sare ou d'Akelarren-leze à Zugarramurdi où il tenait une école, en concurrence avec les universités de Salamanque et d'autres lieux, et enseignait en peu de temps les sciences, les arts et les lettres",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tinggal di gua Leiza di Sare atau Akelarren-leze di Zugarramurdi, tempat ia menjalankan sekolah yang menyaingi universitas Salamanca dan mengajarkan ilmu, seni, dan sastra dalam waktu singkat.",
        "en": "He lived in the Leiza cave at Sare or Akelarren-leze at Zugarramurdi, where he ran a school rivalling the universities of Salamanca and elsewhere, teaching sciences, arts and letters in a short time."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c08",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Dans ces grottes, Axular, son frère et d'autres compagnons ont effectué leurs études.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di gua-gua itu Axular, saudaranya, dan teman-teman lain konon belajar.",
        "en": "Axular, his brother and other companions are said to have studied in these caves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c09",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Le paiement que ce dernier exigeait de ses disciples pour l'enseignement et le logement, à la fin de leurs études, était que l'un d'eux devait rester pour toujours comme son captif",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai bayaran pelajaran dan penginapan, di akhir pelajaran Etsai menuntut salah satu murid tinggal selamanya sebagai tawanannya.",
        "en": "As payment for teaching and lodging, at the end of their studies Etsai demanded that one pupil stay with him forever as his captive."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c10",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Selon la version d'Ordizia, Atarrabi était le bon fils de Marimundi et avec son frère cadet Mikelats, ainsi que d'autres, ils allèrent étudier à l'école d'Etsai.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi Ordizia, Atarrabi, putra baik Marimundi, bersama adiknya Mikelats dan yang lain belajar di sekolah Etsai.",
        "en": "In the Ordizia version, Atarrabi, the good son of Marimundi, went with his younger brother Mikelats and others to study at Etsai's school."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c11",
      "source_id": "etsai-s1",
      "quote": "Mais Atarrabi eut pitié de son frère et resta à sa place",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Undian jatuh pada saudara Atarrabi, tetapi Atarrabi kasihan kepadanya dan tinggal menggantikannya.",
        "en": "The lot fell to Atarrabi's brother, but Atarrabi took pity on him and stayed in his place."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "etsai-c12",
      "source_id": "etsai-s3",
      "quote": "Lurpeko irelu honi Iraunsuge ere deitu izan diote.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Basque, roh bawah tanah ini juga disebut Iraunsuge.",
        "en": "According to the Basque article, this underground spirit has also been called Iraunsuge."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Gatipedro adalah kucing putih bertanduk satu dari cerita rakyat Galisia yang pada malam hari menuangkan air dari tanduknya sehingga anak-anak mengompol.",
    "en": "The gatipedro is a one-horned white cat of Galician folklore that pours water from its horn at night so children wet their beds.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gatipedro adalah kucing putih bertanduk satu dalam cerita rakyat Galisia. Pada malam hari ia masuk ke kamar anak dan menuangkan air dari tanduknya; anak yang tidur bermimpi buang air kecil lalu mengompol. Karena berjalan dengan empat kaki dan lidahnya, ia dapat diusir dengan garam di depan pintu dan jendela.",
      "en": "The gatipedro is a one-horned white cat of Galician folklore. At night it enters children's rooms and pours water from its horn; the sleeping child dreams of peeing and wets the bed. Since it walks on four legs and its tongue, salt at the door and window drives it off.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gatipedro-c01",

      "en": "Álvaro Cunqueiro described it in Escola de menciñeiros (1960) and used it in Os outros feirantes (1971); in 2018 Jacobo Paz's tale O gatipedro was published.",

      "quote": "The gatipedro is a legendary creature in Galician folklore, who makes children wet their beds. It is portrayed as a white cat with a single brown horn on its head.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gatipedro adalah makhluk legendaris cerita rakyat Galisia yang membuat anak mengompol, digambarkan sebagai kucing putih dengan satu tanduk cokelat di kepalanya.",
        "en": "The gatipedro is a legendary creature of Galician folklore that makes children wet their beds, portrayed as a white cat with a single brown horn on its head."

      "id": "gatipedro-c02",
      "source_id": "gatipedro-s3",
      "quote": "O gatipedro é unha figura mitolóxica de Galicia que fai mexar ós nenos na cama. Ten forma de gato branco cun corno mouro na cabeza.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Galisia, gatipedro adalah tokoh mitologi Galisia yang membuat anak mengompol, berwujud kucing putih dengan tanduk gelap di kepalanya.",
        "en": "According to the Galician article, the gatipedro is a mythological figure of Galicia that makes children wet the bed, shaped like a white cat with a dark horn on its head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gatipedro-c03",
      "source_id": "gatipedro-s1",
      "quote": "At night, it enters houses, goes to children's bedrooms and pours water through its horn. The sleeping child dreams he or she is peeing, and so actually wets the bed.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada malam hari ia masuk ke rumah, mendatangi kamar anak, dan menuangkan air dari tanduknya; anak yang tidur bermimpi buang air kecil dan sungguh-sungguh mengompol.",
        "en": "At night it enters houses, goes to children's rooms and pours water from its horn; the sleeping child dreams of peeing and really wets the bed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gatipedro-c04",
      "source_id": "gatipedro-s1",
      "quote": "According to popular tradition, it can be scared away by salt grains at a room's door and beside the window. It walks on all fours as well as its tongue, so when it tastes the salt, it turns around and leaves the kids alone.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut tradisi rakyat, ia dapat diusir dengan butiran garam di depan pintu kamar dan di samping jendela; karena berjalan dengan empat kaki dan lidahnya, begitu mengecap garam ia berbalik dan meninggalkan anak-anak.",
        "en": "By folk tradition it can be scared off with grains of salt at the bedroom door and by the window; since it walks on four legs and its tongue, once it tastes the salt it turns back and leaves the children alone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gatipedro-c05",
      "source_id": "gatipedro-s1",
      "quote": "The gatipedro was described by Galician author Álvaro Cunqueiro in his book Escola de menciñeiros, originally published by Editorial Galaxia in 1960.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The gatipedro was described by the Galician author Álvaro Cunqueiro in Escola de menciñeiros, published by Editorial Galaxia in 1960."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gatipedro-c06",
      "source_id": "gatipedro-s1",

    "id": "Gerixeti adalah bayangan jiwa orang mati yang mengembara dalam mitologi Basque, terutama di beberapa daerah Biscaya.",
    "en": "Gerixeti is the shadow of a wandering dead soul in Basque mythology, especially in parts of Biscay.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gerixeti-c01",

    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gerixeti, dari kata Basque geriza, \"bayangan\", adalah nama untuk penampakan orang mati atau jiwa yang mengembara di beberapa daerah Biscaya; ia juga disebut Gerizpe atau Kerizpe. Gagasan jiwa sebagai bayangan sejalan dengan kepercayaan bahwa bayangan pemburu pengembara dan anjingnya terlihat di bawah cahaya bulan, dan bahwa bayangan penyihir harus dicambuk untuk menghukumnya.",
      "en": "Gerixeti, from Basque geriza, 'shadow', is the name for the apparition of a dead person or wandering soul in parts of Biscay; it is also called Gerizpe or Kerizpe. The idea of the soul as a shadow matches the belief that the wandering hunter's shadow and his dogs can be seen by moonlight, and that a witch is punished by whipping her shadow.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gerixeti-c01",
        "gerixeti-c02",
        "gerixeti-c03",
        "gerixeti-c04",
        "gerixeti-c05",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pandangan ini tampaknya berasal dari konsep Romawi tentang jiwa, berbeda dengan pandangan Basque yang lebih umum yang menyebut jiwa orang mati Argi, \"cahaya\".",
      "en": "This view seems to derive from the Roman idea of the soul, unlike the more common Basque view that calls the souls of the dead Argi, 'light'.",

      "quote": "Gerixeti est le mot basque désignant l'ombre, le fantôme des ancêtres, l'âme des morts est un être dans la mythologie basque",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gerixeti adalah kata Basque untuk bayangan, hantu leluhur, atau jiwa orang mati, salah satu makhluk mitologi Basque.",
        "en": "Gerixeti is the Basque word for the shadow, the ghost of the ancestors, the soul of the dead, a being of Basque mythology."

    {
      "id": "gerixeti-c02",
      "source_id": "gerixeti-s2",
      "quote": "Gerixeti (del basc geriza: 'ombra') és un esperit propi de la mitologia basca, concretament, d'algunes zones de Biscaia, on aquest nom fa referència a l'aparició d'un mort o de l'ànima errant.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Katalan, Gerixeti adalah roh mitologi Basque dari beberapa daerah Biscaya, nama untuk penampakan orang mati atau jiwa yang mengembara.",
        "en": "According to the Catalan article, Gerixeti is a spirit of Basque mythology from parts of Biscay, the name for the apparition of a dead person or a wandering soul."
      }

    {
      "id": "gerixeti-c03",
      "source_id": "gerixeti-s1",
      "quote": "Gerixeti vient du mot basque geriza qui signifie « ombre », ou dans un autre contexte « refuge, cachette, abri, protection, tutelle. »",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gerixeti berasal dari kata Basque geriza, \"bayangan\", atau dalam konteks lain \"tempat berlindung, persembunyian, perlindungan\".",
        "en": "Gerixeti comes from the Basque geriza, 'shadow', or in other contexts 'refuge, hiding place, shelter, protection'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gerixeti-c04",
      "source_id": "gerixeti-s2",
      "quote": "Per tant, l'ens anomenat Gerixeti rep també altres noms: Gereizpea, Gerizpe, Geizpe, Kerizpe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena itu Gerixeti juga disebut Gereizpea, Gerizpe, Geizpe, atau Kerizpe.",
        "en": "So Gerixeti is also called Gereizpea, Gerizpe, Geizpe or Kerizpe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gerixeti-c05",
      "source_id": "gerixeti-s2",
      "quote": "El concepte d'ànima errant en forma d'ombra coincideix amb la creença que a la llum de la lluna (Ilargi) es pot veure l'ombra del caçador errant i els seus gossos. És la idea que per a castigar una bruixa (sorgin) se li ha de fuetejar l'ombra.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konsep jiwa mengembara berwujud bayangan sejalan dengan kepercayaan bahwa di bawah cahaya bulan (Ilargi) bayangan pemburu pengembara dan anjing-anjingnya dapat terlihat, dan bahwa untuk menghukum penyihir (sorgin) bayangannya harus dicambuk.",
        "en": "The idea of the wandering soul as a shadow matches the belief that by moonlight (Ilargi) one can see the shadow of the wandering hunter and his dogs, and that to punish a witch (sorgin) one must whip her shadow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gerixeti-c06",
      "source_id": "gerixeti-s1",
      "quote": "Tout cela nous semble révéler la conception que les Romains avaient des âmes, en opposition avec l'autre conception, bien plus en vigueur dans le pays, et qui désigne les âmes des défunts sous le nom de Argi (lumière).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Semua ini tampaknya mencerminkan pandangan bangsa Romawi tentang jiwa, berbeda dengan pandangan yang jauh lebih umum di negeri Basque yang menyebut jiwa orang mati Argi (cahaya).",
        "en": "All this seems to reflect the Roman view of souls, unlike the far more common Basque view that calls the souls of the dead Argi (light)."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Grand'Goule, \"Mulut Besar\", adalah naga legenda Poitiers yang memangsa biarawati di ruang bawah tanah Biara Sainte-Croix sampai diusir Santa Radegonda.",
    "en": "The Grand'Goule, 'Big Mouth', is a legendary dragon of Poitiers that devoured nuns in the cellars of the Abbey of Sainte-Croix until St Radegund drove it out.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Grand'Goule, \"Mulut Besar\" dalam dialek Poitevin, adalah naga cerita rakyat Poitiers, semacam versi lokal tarasque. Pada masa Santa Radegonda (abad ke-6) ia tinggal di dasar sungai Clain, masuk ke ruang bawah tanah saat air naik, dan memangsa biarawati Biara Sainte-Croix. Radegonda mengusirnya dengan salib kecil, air suci, dan doa, atau menurut versi lain dengan serpihan Salib Sejati, ke sungai.",
      "en": "The Grand'Goule, 'Big Mouth' in Poitevin, is a folk dragon of Poitiers, a sort of local tarasque. In St Radegund's time (6th century) it lived at the bottom of the Clain, entered the cellars when the water rose, and devoured the nuns of the Abbey of Sainte-Croix. Radegund drove it off with a small cross, holy water and prayer, or in another version with the piece of the True Cross, into the river.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c01",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s1",
      "quote": "The Grande Goule or Grande-Goule (Poitevin dialect: Grand'Goule ... is a folklore dragon that continues to be celebrated in the Poitou region (city of Poitiers) in France.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grande Goule atau Grand'Goule (dialek Poitevin) adalah naga cerita rakyat yang masih dirayakan di Poitou (kota Poitiers), Prancis.",
        "en": "The Grande Goule or Grand'Goule (Poitevin dialect) is a folk dragon still celebrated in Poitou (the city of Poitiers), France."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c02",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s1",
      "quote": "The Grand'Goule is considered to be a local version of the tarasque.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Grand'Goule dianggap versi lokal tarasque.",
        "en": "The Grand'Goule is considered a local version of the tarasque."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c03",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s2",
      "quote": "La bête, décrite comme un dragon monstrueux, aurait vécu au temps de sainte Radegonde, donc au VIe siècle de notre ère. Elle vivait au fond du Clain, et, lors de la montée des eaux, entrait dans les caves labyrinthiques qui traversaient le sol poitevin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, binatang ini naga mengerikan dari masa Santa Radegonda (abad ke-6) yang tinggal di dasar sungai Clain dan saat air naik memasuki ruang bawah tanah berliku di bawah Poitiers.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the beast was a monstrous dragon of the time of St Radegund (6th century) that lived at the bottom of the Clain and, when the water rose, entered the maze of cellars under Poitiers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c04",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s1",
      "quote": "According to legend, the monster haunted the cellars of the Abbey of Sainte-Croix at Poitiers, and used to devour the nuns who descended there, until St. Radegund expelled it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, monster itu menghantui ruang bawah tanah Biara Sainte-Croix di Poitiers dan memangsa para biarawati yang turun ke sana sampai Santa Radegonda mengusirnya.",
        "en": "By legend the monster haunted the cellars of the Abbey of Sainte-Croix at Poitiers and devoured the nuns who went down there until St Radegund drove it out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c05",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s2",
      "quote": "Désireuse d'en finir avec la bête, Radegonde s'arma d'une petite croix, puis d'eau bénite, et, une fois face à face avec la bête, l'aspergea, dit une prière, et la bête disparut dans d'atroces souffrances.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Radegonda membawa salib kecil dan air suci, memercikkannya kepada binatang itu sambil berdoa, dan binatang itu lenyap dalam kesakitan hebat.",
        "en": "Radegund armed herself with a small cross and holy water, sprinkled the beast and prayed, and it vanished in terrible agony."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c06",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s1",
      "quote": "She harnessed the power of the piece of the True Cross held by the Abbey, and by making a sign of the cross, drove the monster out of the cellar into the river.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain menyebut ia memakai kuasa serpihan Salib Sejati milik biara dan dengan tanda salib mengusir monster itu dari ruang bawah tanah ke sungai.",
        "en": "Another version says she used the power of the abbey's piece of the True Cross and, with a sign of the cross, drove the monster from the cellar into the river."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c07",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s1",
      "quote": "What has been claimed to be an early attestation to the Grande Goule is a charter dated 1466 noting that a \"dragon\" was among the banners and ensigns being promenaded through the streets of Poitiers for the Rogation days procession.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bukti awal yang diajukan adalah piagam tahun 1466 yang mencatat \"naga\" di antara panji yang diarak di Poitiers saat prosesi Hari Rogasi.",
        "en": "A claimed early attestation is a charter of 1466 noting a 'dragon' among the banners paraded through Poitiers in the Rogation days procession."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c08",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s1",
      "quote": "A sculpture by cabinetmaker Jean Gargot in 1677, commissioned  by the Abbey of Sainte-Croix at Poitiers, has survived into the modern age, and now housed in the Musée Sainte-Croix, in Poitiers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patung karya tukang kayu Jean Gargot tahun 1677, pesanan Biara Sainte-Croix, masih ada dan kini disimpan di Musée Sainte-Croix, Poitiers.",
        "en": "A sculpture by the cabinetmaker Jean Gargot from 1677, commissioned by the Abbey of Sainte-Croix, survives and is now in the Musée Sainte-Croix, Poitiers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c09",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s2",
      "quote": "Le dragon était promené dans les rues, et la tradition voulait que les enfants lui jettent des petits gâteaux – appelés casse-museaux – en disant cette prière : « Boune sainte veurmine, priez pour nous ! »",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga itu diarak di jalan, dan anak-anak melemparkan kue kecil yang disebut casse-museau kepadanya sambil berdoa \"Boune sainte veurmine, priez pour nous!\".",
        "en": "The dragon was paraded through the streets, and children threw little cakes called casse-museaux at it, praying 'Boune sainte veurmine, priez pour nous!'"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c10",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s2",
      "quote": "Cette procession s'est arrêtée au XIXe siècle",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Prosesi ini berhenti pada abad ke-19.",
        "en": "The procession ended in the 19th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "grande-goule-c11",
      "source_id": "grande-goule-s3",
      "quote": "グラン・グール（ポワトゥー方言: Grand'Goule、「大口」・「大きな獣の口」の意。 Grande Goule、Grande-Gouleとも）は、フランス・ポワトゥー州（ポワティエ市）の伝承におけるドラゴン。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Jepang juga menyebut Grand'Goule, \"mulut besar\", naga dalam tradisi Poitou (Poitiers), Prancis.",
        "en": "The Japanese article also calls the Grand'Goule, 'big mouth', a dragon of the lore of Poitou (Poitiers), France."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Groac'h adalah peri air Breton, sering tua dan mirip penyihir, yang tinggal di gua dan di bawah laut, dapat berubah wujud, dan dikenal jahat lewat cerita Émile Souvestre.",
    "en": "The groac'h is a Breton water fairy, often old and witch-like, living in caves and under the sea, able to change shape, and known as malevolent through Émile Souvestre's story.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "groac-h-c01",
      "groac-h-c02",
      "groac-h-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Groac'h, kata Breton untuk peri, penyihir, atau nenek tua, adalah peri air Bretagne yang muncul dalam berbagai wujud, sering pada malam hari, kadang bergigi walrus. Ia tinggal di gua, di bawah pantai, dan di bawah laut, berkuasa atas alam dan dapat berubah wujud. Groagez paling sering dijumpai di hutan dan dekat mata air sebagai peri sumur, dan kisah lain menyebut mereka peri tua penyendiri yang murah hadiah.",
      "en": "The groac'h, Breton for fairy, witch or crone, is a water fairy of Brittany appearing in many forms, often at night, sometimes with walrus teeth. It lives in caves, under the beach and under the sea, commands nature and can shift shape. The groagez are met most often in forests and by springs as fairies of wells, and other tales make them lonely old fairies generous with gifts.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "groac-h-c01",
        "groac-h-c02",
        "groac-h-c03",
        "groac-h-c05",
        "groac-h-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Citra jahatnya terutama berasal dari cerita Émile Souvestre tentang groac'h di Kepulauan Glénan yang mengubah lelaki menjadi ikan; menurut Joseph Rio, penyamaannya dengan peri lebih karena cerita itu daripada tradisi rakyat. Nama tempat seperti pulau Groix dan mercusuar La Vieille terkait dengannya, dan asal-usulnya dicari pada dewi kuno yang dijadikan setan. Ia muncul dalam novel La Pâleur et le Sang (1983).",
      "en": "Its wicked image comes mostly from Émile Souvestre's tale of a groac'h on the Glénan Islands who turns men into fish; Joseph Rio holds that its equation with the fairy owes more to that tale than to folk tradition. Place names such as the island of Groix and the La Vieille lighthouse are linked to it, and its origin is sought in demonized ancient goddesses. It appears in the novel La Pâleur et le Sang (1983).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "groac-h-c04",
        "groac-h-c06",
        "groac-h-c07",
        "groac-h-c08",
        "groac-h-c09",
        "groac-h-c10"
      ]
    }

      "title": "Qroak'h",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c01",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "A groac'h (Breton for \"fairy\", \"witch\" or \"crone\", pl. groagez) is a kind of Breton water-fairy.  Seen in various forms, often by night, many are old, similar to ogres and witches, sometimes with walrus teeth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Groac'h (bahasa Breton untuk \"peri\", \"penyihir\", atau \"nenek tua\"; jamak groagez) adalah sejenis peri air Breton, terlihat dalam berbagai wujud, sering pada malam hari, banyak yang tua, mirip raksasa pemakan manusia dan penyihir, kadang bergigi walrus.",
        "en": "A groac'h (Breton for 'fairy', 'witch' or 'crone'; plural groagez) is a kind of Breton water fairy, seen in various forms, often at night, many old, like ogres and witches, sometimes with walrus teeth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c02",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "Supposed to live in caverns, under the beach and under the sea, the groac'h has power over the forces of nature and can change its shape.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia konon tinggal di gua, di bawah pantai, dan di bawah laut, berkuasa atas kekuatan alam, dan dapat berubah wujud.",
        "en": "It is said to live in caves, under the beach and under the sea, with power over the forces of nature and the ability to change shape."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c03",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "The groagez are the fairies most often encountered in Brittany, generally in forests and near springs: they are essentially the fairies of Breton wells.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Groagez adalah peri yang paling sering dijumpai di Bretagne, biasanya di hutan dan dekat mata air; mereka pada dasarnya peri sumur Breton.",
        "en": "The groagez are the fairies most often met in Brittany, generally in forests and near springs; they are essentially the fairies of Breton wells."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c04",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "It is mainly known as a malevolent figure, largely because of Émile Souvestre's story La Groac'h de l'île du Lok, in which the fairy seduces men, changes them into fish and serves them as meals to her guests, on one of the Glénan Islands.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia terutama dikenal jahat karena cerita Émile Souvestre, La Groac'h de l'île du Lok, tentang peri di salah satu Kepulauan Glénan yang merayu lelaki, mengubah mereka menjadi ikan, dan menyajikannya kepada tamunya.",
        "en": "It is known mainly as malevolent because of Émile Souvestre's story La Groac'h de l'île du Lok, in which the fairy on one of the Glénan Islands seduces men, turns them into fish and serves them to her guests."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c05",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "Other tales present them as old solitary fairies who can overwhelm with gifts the humans who visit them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah lain menggambarkan mereka sebagai peri tua penyendiri yang dapat melimpahi pengunjungnya dengan hadiah.",
        "en": "Other tales show them as old solitary fairies who may shower their visitors with gifts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c06",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "Several place-names of Lower Brittany are connected with the groac'h, especially the names of some megaliths in Côtes-d'Armor, as well as the island of Groix in Morbihan and the lighthouse of La Vieille.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejumlah nama tempat di Bretagne Bawah terkait groac'h, terutama beberapa megalit di Côtes-d'Armor, pulau Groix di Morbihan, dan mercusuar La Vieille.",
        "en": "Several place names of Lower Brittany are linked with the groac'h, notably some megaliths in Côtes-d'Armor, the island of Groix in Morbihan and the La Vieille lighthouse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c07",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "The origin of those fairies that belong to the archetype of \"the crone\" is to be found in the ancient female divinities demonized by Christianity.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal peri-peri dengan arketipe \"nenek tua\" ini dicari pada dewi-dewi kuno yang dijadikan setan oleh agama Kristen.",
        "en": "The origin of these 'crone'-type fairies lies in ancient female divinities demonized by Christianity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c08",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "According to Joseph Rio the assimilation of the groac'h with the fairy is more the result of the influence of Émile Souvestre's tale, and commentaries on it, than a belief deriving from the popular traditions of Lower Brittany",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Joseph Rio, penyamaan groac'h dengan peri lebih merupakan hasil pengaruh cerita Émile Souvestre dan tafsirannya daripada kepercayaan rakyat Bretagne Bawah.",
        "en": "According to Joseph Rio, equating the groac'h with the fairy owes more to Émile Souvestre's tale and its commentaries than to the folk traditions of Lower Brittany."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c09",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s2",
      "quote": "Selon Philippe Le Stum, à l'origine, « groac'h » (\"gwrac'h\" en breton moderne, proche de \"gwreg\" femme/épouse, dont le pluriel est \"gwragez\") semble être le mot breton pour désigner les fées de manière générale.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Philippe Le Stum, groac'h (gwrac'h dalam bahasa Breton modern, dekat dengan gwreg, \"perempuan, istri\") awalnya tampaknya kata Breton untuk peri secara umum.",
        "en": "According to Philippe Le Stum, groac'h (gwrac'h in modern Breton, close to gwreg, 'woman, wife') originally seems to have been the Breton word for fairies in general."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c10",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s1",
      "quote": "The groac'h has several times appeared in recent literary works, such as Nicolas Bréhal's La Pâleur et le Sang (1983).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Groac'h beberapa kali muncul dalam karya sastra modern, seperti La Pâleur et le Sang (1983) karya Nicolas Bréhal.",
        "en": "The groac'h has appeared in recent literature, such as Nicolas Bréhal's La Pâleur et le Sang (1983)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "groac-h-c11",
      "source_id": "groac-h-s3",
      "quote": "Qroak'h — (Breton dilində \"pəri\", \"cadugər\" və ya \"qoca qadın\", cəm halda groagez) — Breton mifologiyasında rast gəlinən su pərisi növüdür.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Azerbaijan juga menyebut groac'h sejenis peri air dalam mitologi Breton.",
        "en": "The Azerbaijani article also calls the groac'h a kind of water fairy of Breton mythology."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Guajona adalah nenek bertudung hitam dari tradisi Cantabria yang pada malam hari mengisap darah anak-anak yang tidur dengan satu giginya yang panjang.",
    "en": "The Guajona is a black-hooded hag of the tradition of Cantabria who sucks the blood of sleeping children at night with her single long tooth.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "guajona-c01",
      "guajona-c02",

      "guajona-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Guajona atau Lumia adalah monster dari tradisi lisan Cantabria, nenek kurus berjubah hitam berkaki burung, berwajah kuning berkutil, bermata kecil berkilau, dengan satu gigi hitam panjang sampai bawah dagu. Ia hanya keluar pada malam hari, diam-diam masuk ke rumah, dan mengisap darah anak serta remaja yang tidur tanpa membunuh mereka, sehingga mereka bangun lemah dan pucat.",
      "en": "The Guajona or Lumia is a monster of the oral tradition of Cantabria, a thin black-cloaked hag with bird's feet, a yellow warty face, small bright eyes and one long black tooth reaching below her chin. She only comes out at night, slips into houses and drinks the blood of sleeping children and youths without killing them, so they wake weak and pale.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "guajona-c01",
        "guajona-c02",
        "guajona-c03",
        "guajona-c04",
        "guajona-c05"

    {
      "id": "Ia salah satu dari sedikit mitos vampir Spanyol; sumber berbeda apakah ia juga menyerang orang dewasa. Namanya bentuk pembesar dari Guaja (Asturias: Guaxa), mungkin dari bahasa Arab wahsh, \"binatang buas\", atau dari bahasa Latin hostem, \"musuh\". Miguel de Unamuno pernah mengutipnya.",
      "en": "She is one of Spain's few vampire myths; sources differ on whether she also attacks adults. Her name is an augmentative of Guaja (Asturian Guaxa), perhaps from Arabic wahsh, 'wild beast', or from Latin hostem, 'enemy'. Miguel de Unamuno quoted her.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "guajona-c01",
      "source_id": "guajona-s2",
      "quote": "La Guajona o Lumia es un monstruo mitológico de la tradición oral de Cantabria.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guajona atau Lumia adalah monster mitologis dari tradisi lisan Cantabria.",
        "en": "The Guajona or Lumia is a mythological monster of the oral tradition of Cantabria."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c02",
      "source_id": "guajona-s2",
      "quote": "La creencia estima que es una vieja delgada tapada de la cabeza a los pies con un manto negro; sus manos son sarmentosas y los pies patas de ave; su cara es amarilla, consumida, rugosa, peluda y con verrugas; sus ojos son diminutos y brillantes como estrellas",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia nenek kurus yang tertutup jubah hitam dari kepala sampai kaki, bertangan kurus berbonggol dan berkaki burung, berwajah kuning, kisut, berbulu, dan berkutil, bermata kecil berkilau seperti bintang.",
        "en": "She is a thin old woman covered head to foot in a black cloak, with gnarled hands and bird's feet, a yellow, wasted, wrinkled, hairy, warty face, and tiny eyes bright as stars."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c03",
      "source_id": "guajona-s2",
      "quote": "la nariz aquilina y la boca provista de un único diente negro, largo y afilado que le llega hasta debajo de la barbilla y utiliza para sorber sangre.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hidungnya bengkok dan mulutnya bergigi tunggal hitam, panjang, dan tajam sampai ke bawah dagu, yang dipakai untuk mengisap darah.",
        "en": "Her nose is hooked and her mouth holds a single long, sharp black tooth reaching below her chin, used to suck blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c04",
      "source_id": "guajona-s2",
      "quote": "Sólo sale de noche ocultándose entre las sombras y se desconoce dónde duerme de día, aunque se sospecha que se esconde bajo tierra.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia hanya keluar pada malam hari, bersembunyi dalam bayangan, dan tidak diketahui di mana ia tidur pada siang hari, meski diduga bersembunyi di bawah tanah.",
        "en": "She only comes out at night, hiding in the shadows; where she sleeps by day is unknown, though she is thought to hide underground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c05",
      "source_id": "guajona-s2",
      "quote": "Entra en las casas sin hacerse notar y se acerca en silencio a los niños y jóvenes sanos dormidos para chuparles la sangre clavándoles en vena su diente, aunque no los mata, sino que los deja casi exangües",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia masuk ke rumah tanpa ketahuan, diam-diam mendekati anak dan remaja sehat yang tidur, lalu menancapkan giginya ke pembuluh darah untuk mengisap darah, tanpa membunuh mereka tetapi membuat mereka hampir kehabisan darah.",
        "en": "She slips into houses unnoticed, quietly approaches healthy sleeping children and youths and sinks her tooth into a vein to suck their blood, not killing them but leaving them almost bloodless."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c06",
      "source_id": "guajona-s2",
      "quote": "No ataca a los viejos o adultos. Se trata de uno de los escasos mitos o leyendas sobre vampiros que existen en España.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tidak menyerang orang tua atau dewasa; ini salah satu dari sedikit mitos vampir di Spanyol.",
        "en": "She does not attack the old or adults; it is one of Spain's few vampire myths."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c07",
      "source_id": "guajona-s1",
      "quote": "Guajona also attacks adults. This is one of the few myths or legends about vampires that exist in Spain, along with the Conde Estruch.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Inggris justru menyebut Guajona juga menyerang orang dewasa, dan bersama Conde Estruch menjadi salah satu dari sedikit legenda vampir di Spanyol.",
        "en": "The English article instead says the Guajona also attacks adults and, with the Conde Estruch, is one of Spain's few vampire legends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c08",
      "source_id": "guajona-s2",
      "quote": "Guajona es un aumentativo de Guaja, presente también en la vecina mitología asturiana como Guaxa. El origen de Guaja o Guaxa podría estar en el árabe clásico وحش wahsh, con el significado de \"fiera\", como sostiene Xosé Lluis García Arias.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guajona adalah bentuk pembesar dari Guaja, yang dalam mitologi Asturias disebut Guaxa; menurut Xosé Lluis García Arias asalnya mungkin bahasa Arab klasik wahsh, \"binatang buas\".",
        "en": "Guajona is an augmentative of Guaja, known in neighbouring Asturian mythology as Guaxa; according to Xosé Lluis García Arias it may come from Classical Arabic wahsh, 'wild beast'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c09",
      "source_id": "guajona-s2",
      "quote": "Xulio Concepción, por su parte, lo relaciona con el latín hostem: \"enemigo\", a su vez derivado del indoeuropeo ghos-ti: \"extranjero, huésped\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Xulio Concepción mengaitkannya dengan bahasa Latin hostem, \"musuh\", dari akar Indo-Eropa ghos-ti, \"orang asing, tamu\".",
        "en": "Xulio Concepción links it instead to Latin hostem, 'enemy', from Indo-European ghos-ti, 'stranger, guest'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guajona-c10",
      "source_id": "guajona-s1",
      "quote": "It is also quoted by Miguel de Unamuno.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga dikutip oleh Miguel de Unamuno.",
        "en": "It is also quoted by Miguel de Unamuno."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hydrus adalah ular air dari bestiari abad pertengahan yang menyusup ke mulut buaya yang tidur lalu merobek perutnya dari dalam, lambang Kristus yang keluar dari neraka.",
    "en": "The hydrus is a water snake of medieval bestiaries that slips into a sleeping crocodile's mouth and tears its belly from inside, an emblem of Christ breaking out of Hell.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hydrus-legendary-creature-c01",
      "hydrus-legendary-creature-c02",
      "hydrus-legendary-creature-c04",
      "hydrus-legendary-creature-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hydrus, juga disebut Enhydros atau Ydrus, adalah makhluk bestiari Abad Pertengahan yang biasanya dianggap ular air, tetapi juga digambarkan sebagai burung, naga, atau luwak. Menurut Aberdeen Bestiary namanya berasal dari kata Yunani untuk air karena ia hidup di Sungai Nil. Plinius Tua menyebut bisanya paling kuat di antara ular, dengan hati sebagai penawarnya, dan Thomas dari Cantimpré menyebut gigitannya menimbulkan \"penyakit boa\".",
      "en": "The hydrus, also called Enhydros or Ydrus, is a creature of Medieval bestiaries usually taken as a water snake but also shown as a bird, dragon or mongoose. The Aberdeen Bestiary derives its name from the Greek for water because it lives in the Nile. Pliny the Elder called its venom the strongest of any snake, its liver the cure, and Thomas of Cantimpré said its bite causes the 'boa sickness'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hydrus-legendary-creature-c01",
        "hydrus-legendary-creature-c02",
        "hydrus-legendary-creature-c03",
        "hydrus-legendary-creature-c06",
        "hydrus-legendary-creature-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hydrus membenci buaya: ia berlumur lumpur, menyelinap ke mulut buaya yang tidur, lalu merobek perutnya dari dalam. Tradisi Kristen menjadikannya lambang Kristus yang turun ke dunia orang mati dan bangkit, seperti dalam Bestiaire de Gervaise. Ia kadang dicampuradukkan dengan Hydra dari mitos Yunani.",
      "en": "The hydrus hates crocodiles: it coats itself in mud, slips into a sleeping crocodile's mouth and rips open its belly from inside. Christian tradition made it a symbol of Christ descending to the dead and rising, as in the Bestiaire de Gervaise. It is sometimes confused with the Hydra of Greek myth.",
      "claim_ids": [

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c01",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "The Hydrus (also known as Enhydros, Enidros, Hildris, Hydra, Hidri, Idra, Idres, Ydre, Ydris, and Ydrus) is a creature from Medieval bestiaries.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hydrus (juga Enhydros, Enidros, Hildris, Hydra, Hidri, Idra, Idres, Ydre, Ydris, dan Ydrus) adalah makhluk dari bestiari Abad Pertengahan.",
        "en": "The Hydrus (also Enhydros, Enidros, Hildris, Hydra, Hidri, Idra, Idres, Ydre, Ydris and Ydrus) is a creature of Medieval bestiaries."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c02",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "A hydrus has no set appearance. Normally it is considered to be a water snake. Other animals the hydrus has been thought of as include a bird, a dragon, and a mongoose.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wujud hydrus tidak tetap; biasanya ia dianggap ular air, tetapi juga digambarkan sebagai burung, naga, atau luwak.",
        "en": "The hydrus has no fixed look; it is usually taken to be a water snake, but also shown as a bird, a dragon or a mongoose."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c03",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "Pliny the Elder described the hydrus as a amphibious serpent with stronger venom than any other snake. He also says that the hydrus' liver can be used as an antidote to its bite.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Plinius Tua menggambarkan hydrus sebagai ular amfibi dengan bisa lebih kuat dari ular mana pun, dan hatinya dapat dipakai sebagai penawar gigitannya.",
        "en": "Pliny the Elder described the hydrus as an amphibious serpent with venom stronger than any other snake's, whose liver can serve as an antidote to its bite."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c04",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "They were said to be found in the Nile River. It is said that the hydrus hated crocodiles, and would intentionally roll in the mud and seek out a crocodile while it slept with its mouth open.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hydrus konon hidup di Sungai Nil dan membenci buaya; ia sengaja berguling di lumpur lalu mencari buaya yang tidur dengan mulut terbuka.",
        "en": "They were said to live in the Nile and to hate crocodiles; the hydrus would roll in mud on purpose and seek out a crocodile sleeping with its mouth open."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c05",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s1",
      "quote": "Once inside the crocodile's stomach, the hydrus would burst free from the stomach lining, ripping out the crocodile's entrails and leaving it to die. In medieval Christian tradition, this bursting free from the crocodile became an emblem of the resurrection of Christ bursting free from Hell.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di dalam perut buaya, hydrus merobek dinding perut dan isi perut buaya itu hingga mati; dalam tradisi Kristen abad pertengahan, hal ini menjadi lambang kebangkitan Kristus yang keluar dari neraka.",
        "en": "Inside the crocodile's stomach the hydrus tears free through the lining and entrails, leaving it to die; in medieval Christian tradition this became an emblem of Christ's resurrection breaking out of Hell."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c06",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s2",
      "quote": "Selon Thomas de Cantimpré, au XIIIe siècle, la morsure cause un effet de sidération, nommée la \"maladie du boa\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Thomas dari Cantimpré pada abad ke-13, gigitannya menimbulkan efek lumpuh yang disebut \"penyakit boa\".",
        "en": "According to Thomas of Cantimpré in the 13th century, its bite causes a stunning effect called the 'boa sickness'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c07",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s2",
      "quote": "Dans le Bestiaire de Gervaise, on retrouve la mention de ce récit, avec l'interprétation théologique du Christ descendu aux Enfers pour sauver les âmes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bestiaire de Gervaise memuat kisah ini dengan tafsiran teologis tentang Kristus yang turun ke dunia orang mati untuk menyelamatkan jiwa-jiwa.",
        "en": "The Bestiaire de Gervaise tells this story with the theological reading of Christ descending to Hell to save souls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c08",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s2",
      "quote": "Certaines descriptions peuvent le mélanger avec d'autres serpents aquatiques ou avec l'hydre issue des mythes grecs.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian gambaran mencampurkannya dengan ular air lain atau dengan Hydra dari mitos Yunani.",
        "en": "Some descriptions mix it with other water snakes or with the Hydra of Greek myth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-c09",
      "source_id": "hydrus-legendary-creature-s3",
      "quote": "За переказами з Абердинського бестіарію, ця істота живе в річці Нілі і називається гідром, позаяк живе в воді (гідро — з грец. «вода»).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Aberdeen Bestiary, makhluk ini hidup di Sungai Nil dan dinamai hydrus karena hidup di air (hydro, bahasa Yunani \"air\").",
        "en": "According to the Aberdeen Bestiary this creature lives in the Nile and is called hydrus because it lives in water (hydro, Greek 'water')."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

    "id": "Ichneumon adalah hewan dalam sastra abad pertengahan, musuh naga yang melumuri diri dengan lumpur lalu menyerang naga, buaya, dan ular aspis.",
    "en": "The ichneumon is an animal of medieval literature, the dragon's enemy, which coats itself in mud and then attacks dragons, crocodiles and asps.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c01",
      "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c02"

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ichneumon, dari bahasa Yunani \"pelacak\", adalah nama luwak Mesir yang dalam sastra abad pertengahan menjadi musuh naga. Ia melumuri diri dengan lumpur, menutup hidung dengan ekornya, lalu menyerang dan membunuh naga; ia juga melawan buaya dan ular aspis dengan cara sama. Bentuknya seperti musang ferret yang ramping, cepat, dan pandai melompat.",
      "en": "The ichneumon, Greek for 'tracker', was a name for the Egyptian mongoose that medieval literature turned into the dragon's enemy. It coats itself in mud, plugs its nose with its tail, and attacks and kills dragons; it fights crocodiles and asps the same way. It looks like a slim ferret, quick and a good jumper.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c01",
        "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c02",
        "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c03",
        "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c07",
        "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Plinius Tua menulis bahwa ia berlapis lumpur kering sebelum bertempur, dan Strabo menyebutnya masuk ke mulut buaya yang berjemur lalu keluar setelah memakan isi perutnya; penduduk Herakleopolis memujanya. Lewat penulis kuno ini, sastra Eropa membuatnya melawan basilisk dan cacatrix.",
      "en": "Pliny the Elder wrote that it armours itself in dried mud before battle, and Strabo that it enters a basking crocodile's mouth and comes out after eating its insides; the people of Heracleopolis worshipped it.       "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s2",
      "url": "https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ichneumon_(zoologie_m%C3%A9di%C3%A9vale)",
      "title": "Ichneumon (zoologie médiévale)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (fr)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "fr",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s3",
      "url": "https://ca.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ichneumon",
      "title": "Ichneumon",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ca)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ca",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c01",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "In medieval literature, the ichneumon or echinemon was the enemy of the dragon. When it sees a dragon, the ichneumon covers itself with mud, and closing its nostrils with its tail, attacks and kills the dragon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sastra abad pertengahan, ichneumon atau echinemon adalah musuh naga: ketika melihat naga, ia melumuri diri dengan lumpur, menutup lubang hidungnya dengan ekor, lalu menyerang dan membunuh naga itu.",
        "en": "In medieval literature the ichneumon or echinemon was the dragon's enemy: on seeing a dragon it coats itself in mud, closes its nostrils with its tail, and attacks and kills the dragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c02",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "The ichneumon was also considered by some to be the enemy of the crocodile and the asp, and attacks them in the same way. The name was used for the pharaoh's rat, mongoose, or Egyptian mongoose (Herpestes ichneumon), which attacks snakes; it can also mean otter.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian penulis juga menganggapnya musuh buaya dan ular aspis yang diserang dengan cara sama; nama ini dipakai untuk luwak Mesir (Herpestes ichneumon) yang menyerang ular, dan juga dapat berarti berang-berang.",
        "en": "Some also thought it the enemy of the crocodile and the asp, attacking them the same way; the name was used for the Egyptian mongoose (Herpestes ichneumon), which attacks snakes, and can also mean otter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c03",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "Ichneumon (ἰχνεύμων) means \"tracker\" in Greek.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ichneumon (ἰχνεύμων) berarti \"pelacak\" dalam bahasa Yunani.",
        "en": "Ichneumon (ἰχνεύμων) means 'tracker' in Greek."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c04",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "It plunges itself repeatedly into the mud, and then dries itself in the sun: as soon as, by these means, it has armed itself with a sufficient number of coatings, it proceeds to the combat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Plinius Tua, ia berulang kali mencelupkan diri ke lumpur lalu mengeringkannya di bawah matahari, dan setelah berlapis cukup tebal ia maju bertempur.",
        "en": "According to Pliny the Elder it plunges repeatedly into mud and dries in the sun, and once armed with enough coatings it goes into battle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c05",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "They lie in wait for the crocodiles, when the latter are basking in the sun with their mouths open; they then drop into their jaws, and eating through their intestines and belly, issue out of the dead body.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Strabo, ichneumon menunggu buaya yang berjemur dengan mulut terbuka, masuk ke rahangnya, lalu memakan usus dan perutnya hingga keluar dari bangkainya.",
        "en": "According to Strabo, ichneumons wait for crocodiles basking with open mouths, drop into their jaws, and eat through the guts and belly to come out of the dead body."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c06",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "but the Heracleotæ worship the ichneumon, which is most destructive both to crocodiles and asps.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Strabo mencatat penduduk Herakleopolis memuja ichneumon, pembasmi buaya dan ular aspis.",
        "en": "Strabo records that the people of Heracleopolis worship the ichneumon, the great destroyer of crocodiles and asps."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c07",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s1",
      "quote": "The ichneumon is shaped similarly to a ferret, although much slimmer in its shape, and the head is elongated. Also, it is an animal that can move swiftly and is able to jump a couple of yards with a single leap.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuknya mirip musang ferret tetapi jauh lebih ramping dengan kepala memanjang, bergerak cepat dan dapat melompat beberapa meter sekali loncat.",
        "en": "It is shaped like a ferret but much slimmer, with an elongated head, moves swiftly and can leap a couple of yards in one bound."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c08",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s2",
      "quote": "Par l’intermédiaire des chroniqueurs antiques, notamment Pline l’Ancien, il sera ponctuellement associé à d’autres animaux dans la littérature médiévale européenne où il affrontera des dragons comme le basilic ou la cacatrix.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, lewat penulis kuno seperti Plinius Tua, dalam sastra Eropa abad pertengahan ia dikaitkan dengan hewan lain dan melawan naga seperti basilisk atau cacatrix.",
        "en": "According to the French article, through ancient writers such as Pliny the Elder it was linked in medieval European literature with other animals and fought dragons such as the basilisk or the cacatrix."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c09",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s2",
      "quote": "L’animal était utilisé par les Égyptiens et les indiens dans les périodes anciennes pour se débarrasser des serpents venimeux, comme le cobra, ou encore d’autres reptiles comme les crocodiles.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hewan ini dipakai orang Mesir dan India pada masa kuno untuk menyingkirkan ular berbisa seperti kobra dan reptil lain seperti buaya.",
        "en": "The animal was used by Egyptians and Indians in ancient times to get rid of venomous snakes such as the cobra and other reptiles such as crocodiles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-c10",
      "source_id": "ichneumon-medieval-zoology-s3",
      "quote": "En la literatura medieval, l'ichneumon o echinemon era l'enemic del drac.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Katalan juga menyebut ichneumon atau echinemon musuh naga dalam sastra abad pertengahan.",
        "en": "The Catalan article likewise calls the ichneumon or echinemon the enemy of the dragon in medieval literature."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, fr, dan ca; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Idittu adalah roh malam usil mitologi Basque, sering berwujud babi atau burung penyembur api, yang menuntun orang penasaran ke jurang dan tempat berbahaya.",
    "en": "The idittu is a mischievous night spirit of Basque mythology, often a pig or a fire-breathing bird, that leads the curious into ravines and dangerous places.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "idittu-c01",
      "idittu-c02",
      "idittu-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Idittu, juga Ireltxu dan salah satu nama iratxo, adalah roh malam usil mitologi Basque yang terutama dikenal di Gipuzkoa dan Biscaya. Ia tidak jahat, tetapi menuntun orang yang penasaran ke jurang dan tebing tempat iblis berdiam, dan menyesatkan mereka dengan mengubah cahaya bulan.",
      "en": "The idittu, also Ireltxu and one of the iratxo's names, is a mischievous night spirit of Basque mythology known mainly in Gipuzkoa and Biscay. It is not evil, but leads the curious to ravines and cliffs where the devil lives, and misleads them by changing the moonlight.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "idittu-c01",
        "idittu-c02",
        "idittu-c03",
        "idittu-c04",
        "idittu-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia muncul sebagai manusia, keledai, domba hitam, kuda, kucing, dan paling sering babi; Azkue dan Barandiarán menggambarkannya sebagai burung yang menyemburkan api seperti naga. Dekat Gautegiz Arteaga ia tampak sebagai nyala api dari tanah, mungkin api pelita.",
      "en": "It appears as a human, donkey, black ram, horse, cat and most often a pig; Azkue and Barandiarán described it as a bird breathing fire like a dragon. Near Gautegiz Arteaga it showed as flames from the ground, perhaps will-o'-the-wisps.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "idittu-c05",
        "idittu-c06",
        "idittu-c07",
        "idittu-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],
